# Rashi on Judges

רש"י על שופטים

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges)

הפירוש הנפוץ והמוכר ביותר לתורה, המבאר את פשטי המקראות בתוספת הרחבות פרשניות; חובר במאה ה-11.

Most widely-read biblical commentary, compiled in the 11th-century, explaining the simple meaning of the text with interpretive elaborations.

Sections: Chapter-Verse-Comment

Location: Tanakh/Rishonim on Tanakh/Rashi/Prophets

Originally from [Sefaria](https://www.sefaria.org) via [TorahLM.org](https://torahlm.org) — see [licensing](https://torahlm.org/sefaria_license).

Version (He): Sefaria vocalized edition (Segments: 458, License: unknown)

Version (En): The Metsudah Tanach series, Lakewood, N.J (Segments: 457)


## Chapter 1


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Judges 1:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:1:1)


**מִי יַעֲלֶה לָּנוּ אֶל הַכְּנַעֲנִי.** עַל מְקוֹמוֹת שֶׁהִפַּלְנוּ בְּגוֹרָל וַעֲדַיִן לֹא נִכְבְּשׁוּ:


**Who shall go up for us against the Canaanites?** To the territories for which lots were cast¹ but are as yet unconquered.

footnotes:
¹ By Yehoshua, Elazar, and the tribal chieftains (Yehoshua, 14:1,2). Chapters 14–19 (there) list in detail the territories alloted to the various tribes when the lots were cast.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Judges 1:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:2:1)


**יְהוּדָה יַעֲלֶה.** שֵׁבֶט יְהוּדָה יַעֲלֶה תְּחִלָּה לִכְבֹּשׁ אֶת גּוֹרָלוֹ:


**Let Yehudah go up.** Let the tribe of Yehudah go up first² to conquer their alloted territory.

footnotes:
² The tribesmen of Yehudah were first in many instances; they entered the water first at the splitting of the sea, their banner was the first to proceed when the Yisroelites journeyed in the wilderness, they went up first in the Book of Shoftim. Indeed, at the coming redemption, their tribesman Dovid will reign as the first of his countrymen. (Yalkut, 37:a. See Yalkut to Beshalach, 234)



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on Judges 1:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:3:1)


**לְשִׁמְעוֹן אָחִיו.** לְשֵׁבֶט שִׁמְעוֹן:


**To his brother Shimon.** To the tribe of Shimon.


###### Rashi on Judges 1:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:3:2)


**עֲלֵה אִתִּי.** וְעָזְרֵנִי לִכְבּוֹשׁ אֶת מַה שֶׁנָּפַל בְּגוֹרָלִי וְיֵשׁ פּוֹתְרִין: יְהוּדָה יַעֲלֶה, הוּא עָתְנִיאֵל, הוּא יַעְבֵּץ, שֶׁאָנוּ אוֹמְרִים בְּמַסֶּכֶת תְּמוּרָה (טז א): מַה שְּׁמוֹ, יְהוּדָה אֲחִי שִׁמְעוֹן שְׁמוֹ, וּלְשִׁמְעוֹן אָחִיו אָמַר שֶׁיֵּלֵךְ אִתּוֹ:


**Go up with me** and assist me in conquering my alloted portion. Some interpret "Let Yehudah go up" as a reference to Asniel,³ also known as Ya'avetz;⁴ for it is remarked, in tractate Temurah,⁵ "What was his name? His name was Yehudah, brother of Shimon."⁶ He⁷ told his brother Shimon⁸ to accompany him.

footnotes:
³ Asniel ben Kenaz, the first of the Shoftim to lead Yisroel after the death of Yehoshua. (Below, 3:9–11)
⁴ Ya’avetz is listed among the tribesmen of Yehudah, and described as “revered over his brethren.” (Divrei Hayamim 1, 4:9)
⁶ This was his true name. His surnames were Asniel and Ya’avetz. Asniel is a contraction of ‘anah El’, ‘Adonoy answered’ [Divrei Hayamim 1, 4:10, relates that Adonoy answered his prayer]. Ya’avetz is a contraction of ‘ya’atz v’herebetz’, ‘he counseled and disseminated [Torah]’. (Temurah, 16:a)
⁷ Not the tribe of Yehudah, but the individual Yehudah, otherwise known as Asniel or Ya’avetz.
⁸ The individual Shimon, brother of Yehudah / Asniel. Accordingly, the inquiry “Who shall go up first?” [v.1] did not concern the tribe which would engage in battle first, but the individual who would lead the people into battle. Adonoy answered [v.2] that Yehudah, also known as Asniel or Ya’avetz, would lead.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Judges 1:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:4:1)


**בְּבֶזֶק.** שֵׁם מָקוֹם:


**At Bezek.** The name of a place.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ ‘Bezek’ may also be translated as ‘shard’, meaning a broken piece of pottery. Here, however, the reference is to a place named ‘Bezek’. (Yoma, 22:b)



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on Judges 1:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:7:1)


**שִׁבְעִים מְלָכִים וְגוֹ׳.** מִכָּאן אַתָּה לָמֵד מַה גְּדוּלָּתָן וְעָשְׁרָן שֶׁל מַלְכֵי כְּנַעַן, שֶׁהֲרֵי אֲדֹנִי בֶזֶק לֹא הָיָה כְּדַאי לִמְנוֹת (יהושע יב) עִם מַלְכֵי כְּנַעַן בְּשֵׁם ׳מֶלֶךְ׳, וְכָתוּב בּוֹ כֵּן:


**Seventy kings.** From here we learn of the eminence and affluence of the Canaanite potentates, for Adoni-bezek was not worthy of being listed among the Canaanite monarchs¹⁰ by the title "king", yet this is written of him.¹¹

footnotes:
¹⁰ Yehoshua 12: 7–24 lists the thirty one Canaanite kings. Adoni bezek is not among them.
¹¹ Yalkut, 37:a.


###### Rashi on Judges 1:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:7:2)


**וַיְבִיאֻהוּ יְרוּשָׁלִַם.** שֶׁהָלְכוּ מִשָּׁם לְהִלָּחֵם עַל יְרוּשָׁלַיִם:


**They brought him to Yerushalayim.** They proceeded from there to do battle at Yerushalayim.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on Judges 1:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:8:1)


**שִׁלְּחוּ בָאֵשׁ.** שִׁלְּחוּ אֵשׁ בְּכֻלָּהּ:


**They set [literally 'sent'] fire.** They sent the fire¹² throughout it.

footnotes:
¹² By igniting it. This follows the literal meaning of שׇלְחוּ, ‘thy sent’. (Metzudas Tzion) Afterward, the B’nei Yehudah went down to wage war with the Canaanites dwelling in the hills, the Negev, and the lowlands.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Judges 1:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:13:1)


**אֲחִי כָלֵב.** מֵאִמּוֹ:


**Coleiv's …brother.** Maternally.¹³

footnotes:
¹³ Coleiv is referred to as “the Kenizite” (Yehoshua, 14:6), rather than ‘son of Kenaz’. This indicates that he was not Kenaz’ biological son, but his stepson. (Sotah, 11:b, 12:a).



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on Judges 1:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:14:1)


**וַתְּסִיתֵהוּ.** (תרגום:) וְאַמְלָכְתֵּיהּ, לְשׁוֹן נִמְלָךְ, שֶׁמְּיַעֲצִין הָאָדָם וּמַשִּׂיאִין לִבּוֹ לְדָבָר אַחֵר, נִרְאֶה לִי:


**She cajoled him.** She advised him, as in "he took counsel";¹⁴ one counsels a person, and directs his thinking to an alternative. This is my opinion.

footnotes:
¹⁴ Rather than ‘she tempted him’, as in Devarim, 13:7.


###### Rashi on Judges 1:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:14:2)


**וַתִּצְנַח.** (תרגום:) וְאִתְרְכִינַת, לִיפּוֹל לְרַגְלֵי אָבִיהָ:


**She lowered herself.** "She lowered herself"¹⁵ as if falling at her father's feet.

footnotes:
¹⁵ This is a quotation from Targum Yonasan.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Judges 1:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:15:1)


**אֶרֶץ הַנֶּגֶב.** אָדָם שֶׁבֵּיתוֹ מְנֻגָּב מִכָּל טוּב, שֶׁאֵין בּוֹ אֶלָּא תּוֹרָה:


**Arid land.** A man barren of anything gainful, as he contains nothing but Torah.¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁶ Temurah, 16:a.


###### Rashi on Judges 1:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:15:2)


**גֻּלֹּת מָיִם.** שְׂדֵה בֵּית הַבַּעַל, (תרגום:) אֲתַר בֵּית שָׁקְיָא דְמַיָּא:


**Streams of water.** Wetland, "territory supplied abundantly with water."



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on Judges 1:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:16:1)


**מֵעִיר הַתְּמָרִים.** הִיא יְרִיחוֹ, שֶׁנִּתַּן לָהֶם דּוּשְׁנָהּ שֶׁל יְרִיחוֹ לְאָכְלָהּ עַד שֶׁיִּבָּנֶה בֵּית הַמִּקְדָּשׁ, וּמִי שֶׁיִּבְנֶה בֵּית הַבְּחִירָה בְּחֶלְקוֹ יִטְּלֶנָּה, כְּדֵי שֶׁיְּהֵא לְכָל יִשְׂרָאֵל חֵלֶק בְּבֵית הַבְּחִירָה, וּנְתָנוּהָ לִבְנֵי יִתְרוֹ אַרְבַּע מֵאוֹת אַרְבָּעִים שָׁנָה, וְהַתַּלְמִידִים שֶׁבָּהֶם הִנִּיחוּהָ וְהָלְכוּ אֵצֶל עָתְנִיאֵל בֶּן קְנַז הוּא יַעְבֵּץ בְּמִדְבַּר יְהוּדָה אֲשֶׁר בְּנֶגֶב עֲרָד לִלְמוֹד תּוֹרָה:


**From the city of dates.** This refers to Yericho, as the fertile tract of Yericho¹⁷ by five hundred *amohs*. (Yalkut, 38.) was given to them for their consumption until the Beis HaMikdosh would be built. The one in whose portion the Beis HaMikdosh would be built¹⁸ would then receive it,¹⁹ so that all of Yisroel could share in the Beis HaMikdosh. It was given to the descendants of Yisro for four hundred and forty years.²⁰ The scholars among them departed, and went to Asniel, the son of Kenaz, also known as Ya'avetz, to the wilderness of Yehudah south of Arad to study Torah.²¹

footnotes:
¹⁷ An area outside the city, measuring five hundred amohs
¹⁸ The tribe of Binyomin. (Ibid).
¹⁹ In return for the Beis Hamikdosh territory, which would become the property of all the tribes.
²⁰ From the conquest of Eretz Yisroel by Yehoshua, until the construction of the Beis Hamikdosh by King Solomon. (See Melachim 1,6:1.)
²¹ Yalkut, ibid.


###### Rashi on Judges 1:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:16:2)


**וַיֵּשֶׁב אֶת הָעָם.** עִם הַתַּלְמִידִים הַיּוֹשְׁבִים לְפָנָיו הוּא בִּקֵּשׁ מֵאֵת הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא שֶׁיַּזְמִין לוֹ תַּלְמִידִים הֲגוּנִים, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (דברי הימים א ד:י) וַיִּקְרָא יַעְבֵּץ לֵאלֹהִים וְגוֹ׳, וְזִמֵּן לוֹ הַחֲסִידִים הַלָּלוּ:


**He settled with the people.** With the disciples who sat before him. He requested of the Holy One, blessed is He, that He furnish him with suitable disciples, as it said, "Ya'avetz cried out to Elohim…etc."²² He furnished him with these pious men.²³

footnotes:
²² “…of Yisroel, saying: If You bless me by broadening my boundary, and Your hand shall be with me…”. (Divrei Hayamim 1, 4:10. See fn.6)
²³ Yalkut, ibid.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on Judges 1:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:19:1)


**כִּי לֹא לְהוֹרִישׁ.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: בָּתַר כֵּן דְּחָבוּ, לָא יְכִילוּ לְתָרְכָא יָת יָתְבֵי מֵישְׁרָא:


**However, he could not oust.** [Targum] Yonasan interprets: Afterward, as they had sinned, they could not oust the valley dwellers.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on Judges 1:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:21:1)


**וְאֶת הַיְבוּסִי יֹשֵׁב יְרוּשָׁלִַם.** מָחוֹז הָיָה בִּירוּשָׁלַיִם שֶׁשְּׁמוֹ יְבוּסִי (פרקי דרבי אליעזר לו), וּמִבְּנֵי אֲבִימֶלֶךְ הָיוּ, וְלֹא הוֹרִישָׁם מִפְּנֵי הַשְּׁבוּעָה (בראשית כא:כג-כד) עַד שֶׁבָּא דָוִד (שמואל ב ה:ו-ז), לְפִי שֶׁעֲדַיִן הָיָה נֶכְדּוֹ קַיָּם, וְהוּא נִשְׁבַּע לוֹ וּלְנִינוֹ וּלְנֶכְדּוֹ:


**But the Yevusi dwellers of Yerushalayim.** There was a section of Yerushalayim²⁴ entitled Yevusi. They were descendants of Avimelech,²⁵ and they could not oust them because of the oath—until the arrival of Dovid²⁶—as his²⁷ grandson still lived, and he²⁸ had sworn to him, his son, and his grandson.

footnotes:
²⁴ The reference here is not to members of the Yevusite nation, as Rashi explains that they were descendants of the Pelishte king Avimelech. Cf.Rashi to Yehoshua, 16:63, and Shmuel 2, 5:6.
²⁵ The Pelishite king (Bereishis, 21:32, 26:8). The patriarch Avrohom swore that he would deal kindly with him and his children and grandchildren. (Ibd, 21:23). Avrohom’s descendants were obligated by the oath as well. Cf.Midrash Rabah, Bereishis, 54:2.
²⁶ When Avimelech’s grandchildren no longer survived. (Rashi to Shmueil 2, 5:8)
²⁷ Avimelech’s grandson.
²⁸ Avrohom had sworn.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on Judges 1:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:22:1)


**בֵּית אֵל.** שֶׁנָּפַל בְּגוֹרָלָם (יהושע טז:א), וְאַף עַל פִּי שֶׁפֶּסֶל מִיכָה עִמָּהֶם, שֶׁהֲרֵי מִיכָה מִשֵּׁבֶט אֶפְרַיִם (שופטים יז:א), נֶאֱמַר כָּאן ׳וַה׳ עִמָּם׳, הוּא שֶׁאָמַר דָּנִיֵּאל (דניאל ט:ז): לְךָ ה׳ הַצְּדָקָה וְלָנוּ בֹּשֶׁת הַפָּנִים:


**Beis El** which was part of their ancestral allotment. Although Michah's idol²⁹ was in their possession—for Michah was from the tribe of Ephraim—it is said here, "and Adonoy was with them." Concerning this, Daniel said, "Yours, O Adonoy, is benevolence—ours is the face of humiliation."³⁰

footnotes:
²⁹ Chapter 17, v.4 [below] relates that Michah of Mount Ephraim made a graven image and kept it at his home, where it was worshiped. Although the incident is not mentioned earlier in the text, Rashi asserts that the idol was already in existence during the conquest of Eretz Yisroel. See Rasi to 17:1.
³⁰ Daniel, 9:7.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on Judges 1:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:23:1)


**וַיָּתִירוּ.** עַל יְדֵי אֲחֵרִים, (תרגום:) וּשְׁלָחוּ מְאַלְּלִין, וְיָתוּרוּ אֶת אֶרֶץ (במדבר יג:ב) נֶאֱמַר בַּמְרַגְּלִים עַצְמָן, וְאֵלּוּ שֶׁשָּׁלְחוּ מְרַגְּלִים נֶאֱמַר בָּהֶם וַיָּתִירוּ:


**Sent spies.** Through others, they sent spies.³¹ "They spied the land"³² is said concerning the spies themselves. However, concerning these who sent spies it is said, "they sent spies."

footnotes:
³¹ This is Targum Yonasan’s translation.
³² Bamidbar, 13:21. This refers to the spies sent by Moshe in the wilderness.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on Judges 1:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:24:1)


**אֶת מְבוֹא הָעִיר.** שֶׁהָיוּ נִכְנָסִים לָהּ דֶּרֶךְ הַמְּעָרָה, וְלוּז אֶחָד עוֹמֵד עַל פִּי הַמְּעָרָה וְנִכְנָסִים דֶּרֶךְ הַלּוּז לַמְּעָרָה:


**The entrance to the city.** It was entered through a cave. A hazelnut tree stood at the mouth of the cave, and they passed through the tree³³ into the cave.

footnotes:
³³ The tree was hollow. They passed through the hollow section into the cave, then through the cave into the city. (Bereishis Rabah, 69:8)



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on Judges 1:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:25:1)


**וַיַּרְאֵם.** בְּאֶצְבָּעוֹ:


**He showed them** with his finger.³⁴

footnotes:
³⁴ If this heathen, by the mere flick of a finger, saved himself and his progeny, then certainly one who actively escorts others. (Sotah, 46:b)



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on Judges 1:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:26:1)


**לוּז.** שֶׁגָּדְלוּ בּוֹ אֱגוֹזִים דַּקִּים, קולדר״א בְּלַעַ״ז:


**Luz.** Small nuts grow on this. "Coudre" in old French.


###### Rashi on Judges 1:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:26:2)


**הוּא שְׁמָהּ עַד הַיּוֹם.** לֹא בִּלְבְּלָה סַנְחֵרִיב, וְלֹא הֶחֱרִיבָהּ נְבוּכַדְנֶצַּר:


**It is so entitled to this day.** Sancheriv did not "jumble" it,³⁵ and Nevuchadnetzar did not devastate it.³⁶

footnotes:
³⁵ Sancheriv “jumbled” all the nations of the world by transporting the inhabitants of one country to another. (Yadayim, 4:3, Yeshaya, 10:13). He did the same to the inhabitants of Eretz Yisroel (Melachim 2, 17:6, 23, 24) but not to the inhabitants of Luz.
³⁶ Sotah, 46:b.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on Judges 1:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/1:27:1)


**וְלֹא הוֹרִישׁ מְנַשֶּׁה.** סִפֵּר הַכָּתוּב בִּגְנוּתוֹ, שֶׁהִתְחִילוּ לִמְעוֹל בְּהַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא שֶׁצִּוָּה אוֹתָם (במדבר לג:נב): וְהוֹרַשְׁתֶּם אֶת כָּל יוֹשְׁבֵי הָאָרֶץ וְגוֹ׳:


**Menashe did not oust.** Scripture censures them, as they began betraying the Holy One, blessed is He, Who commanded them: "Oust all those dwelling in the land, etc."³⁷

footnotes:
³⁷ Bemidbar, 33:52.



## Chapter 2


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Judges 2:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:1:1)


**וַיַּעַל מַלְאַךְ ה׳.** שָׁנִינוּ בְּ׳סֵדֶר עוֹלָם׳ (פרק יט): זֶה הָיָה פִּנְחָס:


**Adonoy's emissary went up.** We learn in Seder Olam that this was Pinchos.¹²

footnotes:
¹ Son of Elazar, grandson of Aharon Hakohen. (Bemidbar, 25:11.)
² Why is Pinchas entitled מַלְאַךְ ה׳, lit. “an angel of Adonoy”? Because, when visited by the sacred spirit, he was enflamed with radiance. (Vayikra Rabah, A. See Rashi to Yehoshua, 2:4).


###### Rashi on Judges 2:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:1:2)


**אֶל הַבֹּכִים.** שֵׁם הַמָּקוֹם:


**To Bochim.** The name of a place.³

footnotes:
³ Not literally “weeping”. See vs.4,5.


###### Rashi on Judges 2:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:1:3)


**אַעֲלֶה אֶתְכֶם.** כָּךְ הָיָה דַעְתִּי מִתְּחִלָּה לְהַעֲלוֹתְכֶם מִמִּצְרַיִם, וְכֵן עָשִׂיתִי עַל מְנָת לְהוֹרִישׁ אֶת אוֹיְבַי:


**I took you up.** This was My original intent,⁴ to take you up from Mitzrayim—and I did so—with the stipulation that My enemies be ousted.⁵

footnotes:
⁴ The future tense, אַעֲלֶה, literally “I shall take”, indicates the intent which preceded the actual taking. See Rashi to Shemos, 15:1.
⁵ As indicated by the warning against covenants [v.2].



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on Judges 2:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:3:1)


**וְגַם אָמַרְתִּי.** עַתָּה אָמַרְתִּי בְּאַפִּי:


**Now I have also declared.** At present I have declared, in My fury.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ This is not part of the original intent stated in vs.1,2, but is a new declaration, made now because they sinned.


###### Rashi on Judges 2:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:3:2)


**לְצִדִּים.** לִמְצַדְּדִים בְּצִדְּכֶם בִּגְדוּדִים וְגַיָּסוֹת לִשְׁלֹל וְלָבוֹז:


**At your flanks.** They will harass your flanks with bandits and troops, pillaging and plundering.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on Judges 2:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:6:1)


**וַיְשַׁלַּח יְהוֹשֻׁעַ וְגוֹ׳.** זֶה הַמַּעֲשֶׂה הָיָה מֻקְדָּם, אֶלָּא לְפִי שֶׁבָּא לוֹמַר (פסוק יא): וַיַּעֲשׂוּ הָרַע, הִתְחִיל לוֹמַר כֵּן: יְהוֹשֻׁעַ כְּשֶׁמֵּת, וְקִבְּלוּ לְפָנָיו לַעֲבֹד ה׳, שְׁלָחָם אִישׁ לְנַחֲלָתוֹ, וְהֵם עָבְדוּ אֶת ה׳ כָּל יָמָיו וְכָל יְמֵי הַזְּקֵנִים, וְאַחַר כָּךְ: וַיַּעֲשׂוּ בְּנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל הָרַע:


**Yehoshua dismissed, etc.** This incident had taken place previously, but, as Scripture intends to relate that they acted wickedly [v.11], it begins with the following statement: at Yehoshua's death, when they resolved, in his presence, to serve Adonoy, he dismissed them, each man to his ancestral property. They served Adonoy throughout his days and the days of the elders. Afterward, "The Bnei Yisroel acted wickedly."



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on Judges 2:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:7:1)


**אֲשֶׁר הֶאֱרִיכוּ יָמִים וְגוֹ׳.** יָמִים הֶאֱרִיכוּ, שָׁנִים לֹא הֶאֱרִיכוּ, לְפִי שֶׁנִּתְעַצְּלוּ בְּהֶסְפֵּדוֹ, הוּא שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (פסוק ט): הַר גָּעַשׁ, שֶׁרָעַשׁ עֲלֵיהֶם הָהָר לְהָרְגָן:


**Whose days extended.** Their 'days' extended⁷ but not their 'years', as they neglected to eulogize him.⁸ Thus it is said, "…of Mount Ga'ash"—⁹ the mountain stormed at them¹⁰ to slaughter them.

footnotes:
⁷ In contentment. However, their lives were short. (Shabbos, 105:b.-See Rashi there).
⁸ Scripture omits any mention of a mourning period for Yehoshua, as with Aharon (Bemidbar 20:29) and Moshe (Devarim 34:8). This indicates that they were remiss in eulogizing him and mourning for him. (Tosafos to Shabbos, 105:b).
⁹ “They buried him (Yehoshua)…at Mount Ephraim, north of Ga’ash.” [v.9] Mount Ephraim was better known than G’ash. Why, then, does Scripture identify the famous Mountain by its proximity to the obscure Ga’ash? Evidently, “Ga’ash” is not an identifying landmark, but is intended as a homiletical allusion to “ra’ash”, “storm”, teaching that the mountain stormed against them. (Maharsha to Shabbos, ibid.).
¹⁰ Shabbos, ibid.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on Judges 2:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:9:1)


**בְּתִמְנַת חֶרֶס.** עַל שֵׁם שֶׁהֶעֱמִידוּ תְּמוּנַת חֶרֶס עַל קִבְרוֹ, כְּלוֹמַר חֲבָל עַל אָדָם שֶׁהֶעֱמִיד הַחַמָּה וּמוּטָל בְּקֶבֶר:


**At Timnas Cheres.** So entitled because a likeness of the sun¹¹ was placed over his grave, as if to say, "Alas, that a man who halted the sun¹² lies in the grave."

footnotes:
¹¹ ‘Timnas Serach’ is literally translated as ‘The likeness of the sun’.
¹² Yehoshua ordered the sun to stand still, and the sun obeyed. (Yehoshua, 10:12,13.)



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Judges 2:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:15:1)


**בְּכָל אֲשֶׁר יָצְאוּ יַד ה׳ הָיְתָה בָּם לְרָעָה.** עַל אֲבִימֶלֶךְ וּמַחְלוֹן וְכִלְיוֹן נֶאֱמַר בְּ׳סֵדֶר עוֹלָם׳ (פרק יב):


**Wherever they went, the hand of Adonoy was destructive to them.** This refers to Elimelech, Machlon, and Chilyon—¹³ Seder Olam.

footnotes:
¹³ Naomi’s husband and two sons, mentioned in the opening passages of the Book of Rus. They were punished with poverty and then death. See Rashi to Megilas Rus, ch.1.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on Judges 2:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:16:1)


**וַיָּקֶם ה׳ שֹׁפְטִים.** תָּמִיד מִדּוֹר אֶל דּוֹר, כְּמוֹ שֶׁהוּא מוֹנֶה וְהוֹלֵךְ בַּסֵּפֶר הַזֶּה, שׁוֹפֵט אַחַר שׁוֹפֵט:


**Adonoy established judges.** Continuously, in successive generations, as enumerated in this book, judge succeeding judge.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on Judges 2:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:18:1)


**וְכִי הֵקִים.** לְשׁוֹן הֹוֶה, וְכַאֲשֶׁר הָיָה מֵקִים לָהֶם שׁוֹפְטִים, וְהָיָה ה׳ עִם הַשּׁוֹפֵט וְגוֹ׳:


**When He would establish**—This describes an ongoing process—'When He would establish judges for them, and Adonoy would visit with the judge, etc.'


###### Rashi on Judges 2:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:18:2)


**כִּי יִנָּחֵם.** שֶׁהָיָה מִתְנַחֵם לָהֶם עַל הָרָעָה, כֵּן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן:


**For He would recant.** He would recant, concerning them, from the destruction.¹⁴ This is Yonasan's rendition.

footnotes:
¹⁴ Which He had intended to rain upon them. Accordingly, יְנַחֵם is not understood as ‘would find comfort,’ but as ‘would recant.’ See Rashi to Bereishis, 6:6, second interpretation.


###### Rashi on Judges 2:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:18:3)


**מִנַּאֲקָתָם.** מִפְּנֵי נַאֲקָתָם, הָעוֹלָה לְפָנָיו מִפְּנֵי אוֹיְבֵיהֶם:


**From the outcry**—As a result of their cry that went up to Him because of their tormentors



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on Judges 2:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:19:1)


**לֹא הִפִּילוּ.** לֹא הִנִּיחוּ דָבָר לִהְיוֹת נוֹכָחִים עַל כָּל הַבָּא עֲלֵיהֶם:


**They fell nothing short.** They omitted nothing deserving of the reproach inherent in everything that befell them.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on Judges 2:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/2:22:1)


**הֲשֹׁמְרִים הֵם.** ה״א חֲטַף פַּתַּח הוּא נָקוּד, לְפִי שֶׁהוּא לְשׁוֹן שְׁאֵלָה וְנִסָּיוֹן:


**Whether they observe.** The letter ה is vocalized with the abridged *pasach*,¹⁵ as it expresses the inquiry involved in testing.

footnotes:
¹⁵ This vocalization is used to express inquiry.



## Chapter 3


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Judges 3:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:1:1)


**לְנַסּוֹת בָּם אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל.** אֶת דּוֹר הָאַחֵר אֲשֶׁר לֹא יָדְעוּ אֶת נִסֵּי מִלְחֲמוֹת כְּנַעַן וְלֹא רָאוּ אֶת הַמַּעֲשֶׂה הַגָּדוֹל, וְהִמְרוּ וּמָעֲלוּ בַמָּקוֹם:


**So that Yisroel would be tested through them.** This was a different generation, ignorant of the miracles of the Canaanite wars, and not having witnessed the great deeds, they rebelled, and betrayed the Omnipresent.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Judges 3:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:2:1)


**רַק לְמַעַן דַּעַת.** רַק לְדָבָר הַזֶּה לְבַדּוֹ הִנִּיחָם, לְמַעַן דַּעַת וּלְהִתְבּוֹנֵן דּוֹרוֹת בְּנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל הַבָּאִים מַה הַחֵטְא גּוֹרֵם, כִּי עַתָּה הֵם זְקוּקִים לְלַמְּדָם מִלְחָמָה:


**Only so that they should know.** For this alone, He permitted these nations to remain, so that future generations of the Bnei Yisroel might grasp the consequence of iniquity—for it was now necessary for them to undergo martial training.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:2:2)


**רַק אֲשֶׁר לְפָנִים.** שֶׁהָיוּ עוֹמְדִים לְשָׁרֵת אֶת ה׳:


**Whereas earlier** when they stood worshiping Adonoy.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:2:3)


**לֹא יְדָעוּם.** לְמִלְחָמוֹת הַלָּלוּ וְטַכְסִיסֵיהֶם, וְלֹא נִצְרְכוּ לָהֶם:


**This was unknown.** These wars and the art of battle. It was unnecessary.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on Judges 3:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:3:1)


**חֲמֵשֶׁת סַרְנֵי פְלִשְׁתִּים.** הֵם הַגּוֹיִם אֲשֶׁר הִנִּיחַ לְנַסּוֹת:


**Five Pelishtite sovereignties.** These were the nations whom He permited to remain as a test.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on Judges 3:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:10:1)


**וַתְּהִי עָלָיו רוּחַ ה׳.** דָּרַשׁ רַבִּי תַּנְחוּמָא (מדרש תנחומא שמות כ) נִסְתַּכֵּל בָּהֶם, שֶׁאָמַר הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא לְמֹשֶׁה בְּמִצְרַיִם (שמות ג:ז) רָאֹה רָאִיתִי אֶת עֳנִי עַמִּי, מַה הֵן שְׁתֵּי רְאִיּוֹת הַלָּלוּ, אָמַר לוֹ: רוֹאֶה אֲנִי שֶׁעֲתִידִין לִטְעוֹת בָּעֵגֶל, וְאַף עַל פִּי כֵן ׳רָאִיתִי אֶת עֳנִי עַמִּי׳. זוֹ דָּרַשׁ עָתְנִיאֵל, אָמַר: בֵּין זַכִּים בֵּין חַיָּבִים, עָלָיו לְהוֹשִׁיעָם:


**The Divine spirit visited him.** R' Tanchuma expounded: He studied the statement of the Holy One, blessed is He, to Moshe in Mitzrayim, "I have surely seen (lit. 'seeing, I have seen') the tribulation of my people."¹ What are the two sights?² He said to him, "I see that they are destined to err with the golden calf—nevertheless, 'I have seen the tribulation of my people.'" Asniel expounded upon this, saying, whether innocent or guilty, He is obliged to save them.³

footnotes:
¹ Shemos, 3:7.
² ‘Seeing’ I have ‘seen’.
³ Shemos Rabah, 3:2. Accordingly, “He judged Yisroel” in our passage does not refer to Asniel’s adjudication of litigation in the courtroom, but to his exposition of “I have seen” in his prayer before Adonoy. In his “judgement”, Yisroel was to be saved. (Radal there, 5)



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Judges 3:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:13:1)


**אֶת עִיר הַתְּמָרִים.** יְרִיחוֹ:


**The city of dates.** Yericho.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Judges 3:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:15:1)


**אִטֵּר יַד יְמִינוֹ.** תִּרְגֵּם יְהוֹנָתָן: גְּבַר גְּמִיד יַד יְמִינֵיהּ, וְכָל גְּמִיד לְשׁוֹן אֲרָמִי הוּא, דָּבָר כָּווּץ מֵחֲמַת חֹלִי, ריטרי״ט בְּלַעַ״ז; לֹא הָיָה שׁוֹלֵט בְּיַד יְמִינוֹ. וּבִלְשׁוֹן עִבְרִי: אִטֵּר, אָטוּם, כְּמוֹ (תהלים סט:טז) וְאַל תֶּאְטַר עָלַי בְּאֵר פִּיהָ, אָטוּם בְּיַד יְמִינוֹ, שֶׁלֹּא הָיָה שׁוֹלֵט בָּהּ:


**Whose right arm was immobile.** Yonasan renders "A man whose right arm was shrunken." "Gamid" in the Aramaic always indicates something shrunken by disease, "retrait", "shrunk" in old French. He could not utilize his right arm. In Hebrew, "closed", as in "Let not the well close its mouth over me."⁴ His right arm was "closed"—he could not utilize it.

footnotes:
⁴ Tehilim, 69:16.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on Judges 3:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:16:1)


**גֹּמֶד אָרְכָּהּ.** אַמָּה גְּדוּמָה, וּבִלְשׁוֹן אַשְׁכְּנַז: דוימ״ן לאנ״ג, וּבִלְשׁוֹן לַעַז: גשקור״ט:


**One *gomed* long.** An abridged amoh.⁵ In German, 'daumen lang'. In old French, 'espan courte'.

footnotes:
⁵ The length of the forearm. (Rashbam to Bava Basra, 100:a).


###### Rashi on Judges 3:16:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:16:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:16:2)


**עַל יֶרֶךְ יְמִינוֹ.** לְפִי שֶׁבִּשְׂמֹאלוֹ שׁוֹלֵט, יֹאחֲזֶנָּה בִּשְׂמֹאלוֹ:


**Over his right thigh.** Since he was able to utilize his left hand, he would grasp it with the left hand.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on Judges 3:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:17:1)


**אִישׁ בָּרִיא.** (תרגום:) פָּטִים:


**Obese.** Fat.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on Judges 3:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:18:1)


**וַיְשַׁלַּח אֶת הָעָם.** חָזַר לַאֲחוֹרָיו וְלִוָּה אֶת חֲבוּרַת יִשְׂרָאֵל אֲשֶׁר בָּאוּ עִמּוֹ לָשֵׂאת אֶת הַמִּנְחָה, וְלִוָּה אוֹתָם עַד הַגִּלְגָּל:


**He escorted the people.** He turned back to accompany the delegation of Yisroelites who came with him to bear the gift. He escorted them until Gilgal.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on Judges 3:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:19:1)


**וְהוּא שָׁב.** יְחִידִי:


**He then returned** alone.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:19:2)


**מִן הַפְּסִילִים.** מָקוֹם שֶׁפּוֹסְלִים שָׁם אֲבָנִים מִן הָהָר, וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: מִן מַחְצְבַיָּא, וּבָא אֶל עֶגְלוֹן וַיֹּאמֶר לוֹ:


**From the quarries.** An area where stones were excavated from the mountain. Similarly, Yonasan renders "from the quarries." Then he went to Eglon,⁶ and declared to him.⁷

footnotes:
⁶ Ehud’s return to Eglon is not actually discussed in the text. Rashi mentions the return for the sake of clarification.
⁷ “…I have a matter, etc.”.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:19:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:19:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:19:3)


**וַיֹּאמֶר הָס.** אָמַר עֶגְלוֹן: שִׁתְקוּ אֶת הַכֹּל מֵעָלַי, וְיוֹנָתָן תִּרְגֵּם הָס: סְלִיק, הוֹצִיא כָּל אִישׁ, וְאַף עַל פִּי כֵן ׳הָס׳, לְשׁוֹן שְׁתוֹק:


**He said, "Hsst."** Eglon said, "Silence everyone near me!" Yonasan renders, "Leave!"—remove everyone. Still, the term connotes silence.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Silence them by removing them.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on Judges 3:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:20:1)


**בַּעֲלִיַּת הַמְּקֵרָה.** (תרגום:) בַּעֲלִיַּת בֵּית קַיְטָא, שֶׁהָיְתָה לָאֲוִיר וְצוֹנֶנֶת:


**In his penthouse veranda.** In the summer penthouse which was airy and cool.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:20:2)


**דְּבַר אֱלֹהִים לִי אֵלֶיךָ.** וְצָרִיךְ אַתָּה לַעֲמוֹד:


**I have a Divine message for you!** Thus, you are required to rise.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:20:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:20:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:20:3)


**וַיָּקָם מֵעַל הַכִּסֵּא.** לְכָךְ זָכָה וְיָצְאָה מִמֶּנּוּ רוּת:


**He rose from the throne.** He therefore merited that Rus was descended from him.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on Judges 3:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:22:1)


**הַנִּצָּב.** הוּא הַבַּרְזֶל שֶׁהַשָּׁנוּן תָּחוּב לְתוֹכוֹ, שֶׁקּוֹרִין הול״ט בְּלַעַ״ז, הוּא הַמַּעֲמִיד הַחֶרֶב עַל פִּי תַּעְרָהּ:


**The hilt.** The iron into which the blade is inserted, called 'hulot' in old French. This balances the sword in its scabbard.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:22:2)


**הַלַּהַב.** הוּא הַשָּׁנוּן שֶׁקּוֹרִין ברונ״ט בְּלַעַ״ז, וּבְלַעַ״ז למ״א:


**The blade.** The sharpened section, called "brant" and "lama" in old French.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:22:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:22:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:22:3)


**וַיִּסְגֹּר הַחֵלֶב.** שֻׁמָּנוֹ, שֶׁהָיָה שָׁמֵן מְאֹד, וְכִסָּה אֶת כָּל אֹרֶךְ הַחֶרֶב:


**The fat closed.** His fat. He was extremely corpulent, so that he enclosed the entire length of the dagger.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:22:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:22:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:22:4)


**וַיֵּצֵא הַפַּרְשְׁדֹּנָה.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: וּנְפַק אוּכְלֵיהּ שְׁפִיךְ, עֲשָׂאָן כִּשְׁתֵּי תֵבוֹת וְשִׁי״ן מְשַׁמֶּשֶׁת לִשְׁתֵּיהֶן, וַיֵּצֵא הַפֶּרֶשׁ, שְׁדוֹנָה, בִּשְׁפִיכָה. שָׁפַךְ, ׳שְׁדָא׳ כְּתַרְגּוּמוֹ:


**Waste matter oozed.** Yonasan renders, "Spilled waste matter oozed." He reads this as a contraction of two words with the 'shin' serving both.⁹ "spilled waste matter" [oozed].¹⁰שְׁפַךְ, "spilled", is the translation of שְׁדָא¹¹ according to Yonasan.

footnotes:
⁹ פַּרְשְׁדֹֽנׇה is read as פֶּרֶשׁ־שְׁדוֹנׇא, “spilled waste matter”, with the middle ש serving as the last letter of פֶּרֶשׁ and the first letter of שְׁדוֹנׇא.
¹⁰ The waste matter first spilled from the abdomen into the body, then it oozed from the wound. (Ralbag).
¹¹ Or שְׁדוֹנׇא.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on Judges 3:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:23:1)


**וַיֵּצֵא אֵהוּד הַמִּסְדְּרוֹנָה.** מִן הָעֲלִיָּה אֶל הַמִּסְדְּרוֹנָה, תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: לְאַכְסַדְרָא:


**And Ehud went out to the hallway.** From the penthouse to the מסדרונה, translated by Targum Yonasan as 'hallway'.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:23:2)


**וְנָעָל.** בְּמַפְתֵּחַ:


**And locked** With the key.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on Judges 3:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:24:1)


**וְהוּא יָצָא.** אֵהוּד יָצָא, וְעַבְדָיו שֶׁל עֶגְלוֹן בָּאוּ:


**As he left.** Ehud left, and Eglon's servants came.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:24:2)


**מֵסִיךְ רַגְלָיו.** (תרגום:) עָבֵיד צוֹרְכֵיהּ, הֵם נְקָבִים הַגְּדוֹלִים:


**Relieving himself [lit. 'covering his legs'].** Performing his necessary bodily functions. This refers to a bowel movement.¹²

footnotes:
¹² When the legs are covered as a matter of modesty. (Metzudas).



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on Judges 3:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:25:1)


**עַד בּוֹשׁ.** עַד עִכּוּב, כְּלוֹמַר זְמַן אָרוֹךְ:


**A considerable time.** [lit.] Until [it constituted] delay, as if to say, a considerable time.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on Judges 3:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:26:1)


**נִמְלָט.** בָּרַח, אישקימוציי״ר בְּלַעַ״ז:


**Escaped.** Fled, "eschamocier" in old French.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:26:2)


**עַד הִתְמַהְמְהָם.** כְּמוֹ (שיר השירים א:יב) עַד שֶׁהַמֶּלֶךְ בִּמְסִבּוֹ, (שם ב:ז) אֶת הָאַהֲבָה עַד שֶׁתֶּחְפָּץ, בְּעוֹד שֶׁשְּׁנֵינוּ חֲפֵצִים בָּהּ:


**While [lit. until] they tarried.** As in,¹³ "While the king lounged,"¹⁴ "Affection, while still desired"¹⁵—while we both desire it.

footnotes:
¹³ Rashi cites these quotations to demonstrate that עַד, lit. “until”, may be translated as while”.
¹⁴ Shir Hashirim, 1:12.
¹⁵ Ibid, 2:7.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:26:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:26:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:26:3)


**הַשְּׂעִירָתָה.** יַעַר דַּק וְעָב, שֶׁקּוֹרִין ברוצ״ש בְּלַעַ״ז, וְטוֹב הוּא לְהֵחָבֵא וְהַרְבֵּה יֵשׁ בְּסֵדֶר זְרָעִים שֶׁעַנְפֵי אִילָנוֹת דַּקִּים קְרוּיִין שֵׂעָר (כלאים ד:ט) הוֹפֵךְ אֶת הַשֵּׂעָר, (פאה ב:ג) וְאִם הָיָה הַשֵּׂעָר כּוֹתֵשׁ:


**In the underbrush.** A thin-branched, heavily wooded forest, called "brousaille", brush, in old French, excellent for concealment. Frequently, in the order of Zeraim, thin branches are called "sa'ar"—"[He] overturned the branches,"¹⁶ "If the branches meshed."¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁶ Kilayim, 4:9.
¹⁷ Peah, 2:3.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on Judges 3:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:27:1)


**וַיְהִי בְּבוֹאוֹ.** שֶׁעָבַר אֶת הַיַּרְדֵּן וּבָא לִמְקוֹמוֹ בְּאֶרֶץ כְּנָעַן:


**It happened that, when he arrived.** He crossed the Yardein, and arrived at his place in the land of Canaan.¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁸ The text omits Ehud’s crossing of the Yardein and his arrival in Canaan. Rashi mentions these events for clarification.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:27:2)


**בְּהַר אֶפְרָיִם.** שֶׁהָיָה בְּאֶרֶץ כְּנַעַן בְּעֵבֶר הַיַּרְדֵּן הַמַּעֲרָבִי, וְאֶרֶץ מוֹאָב הָיְתָה בְּעֵבֶר הַיַּרְדֵּן בְּמִזְרָחוֹ, מִתּוֹךְ שֶׁשָּׁלְטוּ בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל וְעָבְרוּ אֶת הַיַּרְדֵּן, יָרְשׁוּ אֶת יְרִיחוֹ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁכָּתוּב לְמַעְלָה (פסוק יג) וְנָתְנוּ אֶת סַרְנֵיהֶם וּשְׁלִיטֵיהֶם עַל יִשְׂרָאֵל:


**At Mount Ephrayim.** Which was in the land of Canaan, on the western side of the Yardein. The land of Moav was on the eastern side of the Yardein, but as they had subdued Yisroel and crossed the Yardein, they occupied Yericho as stated above, and installed their princes and governors over Yisroel.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on Judges 3:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:28:1)


**רִדְפוּ.** לַהֲרֹג אֶת כָּל מוֹאָב שֶׁבְּעֵבֶר הַיַּרְדֵּן שֶׁלָּנוּ:


**Charge behind me** to wipe out all the Moabites on our side of the Yardein.


###### Rashi on Judges 3:28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:28:2)


**וַיִּלְכְּדוּ אֶת מַעְבְּרוֹת הַיַּרְדֵּן.** שֶׁלֹּא יַעַבְרוּ מוֹאָב לָנוּס וְלָשׁוּב אֶל אַרְצָם:


**Took control of the Yardein passes** so that the Moabites could not cross to escape and return to their country.



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on Judges 3:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:29:1)


**כָּל שָׁמֵן.** (תרגום:) כָּל גְּבַר אֵימְתָן:


**All horrific [lit. corpulent].** Every man awe inspiring.



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on Judges 3:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/3:31:1)


**מַלְמַד.** הוּא דָּרְבָן, מַרְדֵּעַ, אגויילו״ן בְּלַעַ״ז:


**With a goad.** A saddle spur, "aiguillon" in old French.



## Chapter 4


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Judges 4:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:2:1)


**בַּחֲרֹשֶׁת הַגּוֹיִם.** שֵׁם עִיר, עַל שֵׁם שֶׁהָיְתָה חֲזָקָה וְשׁוֹלֶטֶת:


**Charoshes Hogoyim.** The name of a city, so entitled because it was a stronghold, and the seat of authority.¹

footnotes:
¹ This is based on Targum Yonasan, who translates חֲרֹשֶׁת הַגּוֹיׅם as “the stronghold, metropolis of nations”.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on Judges 4:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:3:1)


**לָחַץ אֶת בְּנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל בְּחָזְקָה.** דָּרַשׁ רַבִּי תַּנְחוּמָא (צו ב): בְּגִדּוּפִין, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר (מלאכי ג:יג): חָזְקוּ עָלַי דִּבְרֵיכֶם:


**Oppressed the Bnei Yisroel intensely.** R' Tanchuma expounded, through ridicule. Similarly, it is said, "Your remarks offend me intensely."²

footnotes:
² Malachi, 3:13. Vs. 14, 15 there define the offensive remarks as ridicule of the worship of Adonoy.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Judges 4:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:4:1)


**אֵשֶׁת לַפִּידוֹת.** שֶׁהָיְתָה עוֹשָׂה פְּתִילוֹת לַמִּקְדָּשׁ:


**The wife of Lapidos.** She fashioned wicks for the sanctuary.³

footnotes:
³ Megilah, 14:a. לַפּׅיד is translated as “torch”.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on Judges 4:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:5:1)


**תַּחַת תֹּמֶר.** תְּמָרִים הָיוּ לָהּ בִּירִיחוֹ:


**Under [Devorah's] date tree.** She owned date trees in Yericho.


###### Rashi on Judges 4:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:5:2)


**בֵּין הָרָמָה וּבֵין בֵּית אֵל בְּהַר אֶפְרָיִם.** לְפִי תַּרְגּוּמוֹ שֶׁל יוֹנָתָן יֵשׁ לִלְמֹד שֶׁאֵין מִקְרָא זֶה כְּמַשְׁמָעוֹ, וְאֵין אֵלּוּ סִימָנֵי מְקוֹם יְשִׁיבָתָהּ, אֶלָּא לָמַדְנוּ שֶׁאִשָּׁה עֲשִׁירָה הָיְתָה וּמִן הַמְּקוֹמוֹת הָאֵלּוּ הָיְתָה פַּרְנָסָתָהּ, וְהִיא יוֹשֶׁבֶת בְּעִירָהּ, עֲטָרוֹת שְׁמָהּ:


**Between Ramah and Beis El, on Mount Ephrayim.** From Yonasan's translation⁴ we infer that this passage is not meant literally—that these are not intended to identify her dwelling place. Rather, we learn that she was an affluent woman, with financial interests in these places. She lived in a city named Ataros.

footnotes:
⁴ Targum Yonasan renders, “She lived in the city of Ataros. Devorah was financially independent. She owned date trees in Yericho, orchards in Ramah, oil producing olive trees in the wetlands of the Beis Eil plateau, white soil in Tur Malka.”


###### Rashi on Judges 4:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:5:3)


**תַּחַת תֹּמֶר.** תְּמָרִים הָיוּ לָהּ בִּירִיחוֹ, וּכְרָמִים בָּרָמָה, וְזֵיתִים בְּבִקְעַת בֵּית אֵל, שֶׁהוּא מְקוֹם שָׁמֵן, וְעָפָר חִוָּר בְּהַר אֶפְרַיִם, (תרגום:) ׳בְּטוּר מַלְכָּא׳, אוֹמֵר אֲנִי שֶׁמּוֹכְרִין אוֹתָהּ לְיוֹצְרֵי חֶרֶשׂ. וְיֵשׁ פּוֹתְרִין: ׳עָפָר חִוָּר׳, שְׂדֵה זֶרַע, כְּמוֹ (משנה שביעית ב:א): שְׂדֵה הַלָּבָן:


**Under [Devorah's] date tree.** She owned date trees in Yericho, vineyards in Rama, olive trees in the Bethel plateau, a fertile area, and white soil in Mount Ephrayim, at Tur Malka. In my opinion, this was sold for ceramic use. Some interpret "white soil" as grain fields, as in "the grain [lit. "white"] field."⁵

footnotes:
⁵ The Talmud [Moed Katan, 6:b, etc.] refers to the grain field as שֽׂדֵה לׇכׇן, literally “white field.” Rashi [Pe’ah, 3:1] explains that this is because grain whitens when ripe for harvesting. Or, to distinguish between the grain field, which is subject to the sun’s white glare, and the orchard, which is darkened by the shade of the trees.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on Judges 4:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:6:1)


**הֲלֹא צִוָּה.** בְּיַד מֹשֶׁה (דברים כ:יז): כִּי הַחֲרֵם תַּחֲרִימֵם, כָּךְ שְׁנוּיָה בִּמְכִילְתָא (בא יב):


**Has not….commanded?** Through Moshe, "For you shall totally annihilate them."⁶ Mechilta teaches this.⁷

footnotes:
⁶ Devarim, 20:17
⁷ Yalkut, 4:43.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on Judges 4:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:10:1)


**וַיַּעַל בְּרַגְלָיו.** עִמּוֹ:


**At his heels.** With him.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on Judges 4:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:11:1)


**אֵלוֹן בְּצַעֲנַנִּים.** (תרגום:) מֵישַׁר אַגְנַיָּא, מְקוֹם מֵישַׁר שֶׁל בְּצָעִים, מריש״ק בְּלַעַ״ז וְהוּא אַגְנַיָּא גֵּבִים, כְּמִין גּוּמוֹת שֶׁהַמַּיִם נִקְבָּצִים שָׁם, ׳אַגְנֵי דְּאַרְעָא מִקְרוֹן׳ (קידושין סא א):


**Elon Betza'anayim.** The marshland plain.⁸ A plateau area with marshes, "mariscum", "morass" in old French. This is called אַגָנַיָא—pools, similar to ditches where water settles, "entitled 'the earth's flasks.'"⁹

footnotes:
⁸ This is Targum Yonasan’s rendition.
⁹ Kiddushin, 61:a, notes that water filed ditches are entitled אַגׇּנֵי דּֽאַרְעָא, “the earth’s flasks.” Thus, אַגָנַיׇא, “flasks,” refer to the marshland pools.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Judges 4:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:13:1)


**מֵחֲרֹשֶׁת הַגּוֹיִם אֶל נַחַל קִישׁוֹן.** הִזְעִיקָם שֶׁיֵּלְכוּ אֶל נַחַל קִישׁוֹן מֵחֲרֹשֶׁת הַגּוֹיִם שֶׁהוּא יוֹשֵׁב שָׁם:


**From Charoshes Hagoyim to Kishon stream.** He mobilized them to proceed to the stream of Kishon from Charoshes Hagoyim where he was stationed.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on Judges 4:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:18:1)


**בַּשְּׂמִיכָה.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: בְּגוֹנְכָא, וּפֵרַשׁ רַב הַאי (שבת נא א): גּוֹנְכָא - גְּלוּפְקְרִין, קוט״א בְּלַעַ״ז:


**With a cloak.** Yonasan renders גוֹנְכׇא. R' Hai translates גוֹנְכׇא as cloak, "Coate" in old French.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on Judges 4:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:19:1)


**נֹאד הֶחָלָב.** שֶׁהֶחָלָב מַכְבִּיד אֶת הַגּוּף לִהְיוֹת נִרְדָּם:


**The milk pouch.** Since milk induces drowsiness, then slumber.¹⁰

footnotes:
¹⁰ He requested water. Why did she give him milk? To make him drowsy.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on Judges 4:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:20:1)


**עֲמֹד פֶּתַח הָאֹהֶל.** זָרְזִי עַצְמֵךְ כְּזָכָר. וְיֵשׁ פּוֹתְרִים: ׳עֲמֹד׳, כְּמוֹ לַעֲמֹד:


**Stand at the entrance to the tent.** Be zealous about it. Some interpret עֲמֹד [lit. "stand"] as לַעֲמוֹד, "to stand".¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ This interpretation adds the ל, which does not actually appear in the text, so that the reading is “He told her to stand at the entrance.” According to the literal textual interpretation, the reading is, “He told her, stand at the entrance.” This is problematic, as the masculine עֲמוֹד, rather than עִמֽדִי, is inappropriate in addressing a woman. The additional ל solves this difficulty by transforming the command, עֲמוֹד, “stand”, into the infinitive לַעֲמוֹד, “to stand.”



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on Judges 4:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:21:1)


**יְתַד הָאֹהֶל.** קבילי״ל בְּלַעַ״ז, שֶׁתּוֹקְעִין בָּאָרֶץ עִם שִׁפּוּלֵי הָאֹהֶל לְפוֹשְׁטוֹ:


**A tent peg.** "Chevilles" in old French. This is embedded in the ground with the lower flap of the tent in order to extend it fully.


###### Rashi on Judges 4:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:21:2)


**הַמַּקֶּבֶת.** (תרגום:) אַרְזַפְתָּא, מרטי״ל בְּלַעַ״ז:


**A hammer.** Hammer, "martel" in old French.


###### Rashi on Judges 4:21:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:21:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:21:3)


**בַּלָּאט.** (תרגום:) בְּרָז, חֶרֶשׁ:


**Furtively.** Stealthily, in secret.


###### Rashi on Judges 4:21:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:21:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:21:4)


**וַתִּצְנַח בָּאָרֶץ.** (תרגום:) וּנְעָצַת בְּאַרְעָא:


**And impaled it into the ground.**וּנְעַצַתְּ בְּאַרְעָא,¹² and impaled it into the ground.

footnotes:
¹² This is Targum Yonasan’s rendition.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on Judges 4:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/4:22:1)


**בְּרַקָּתוֹ.** (תרגום:) בְּצִידְעֵיהּ, ניל״ה טימפי״ה בְּלַעַ״ז, וּתְחָבָהּ בְּרַקָּתוֹ וּבָאָרֶץ:


**In his forehead.**בְּצִידֽעֵיה, "tenplia" in old French. She had penetrated his forehead and the ground.



## Chapter 5


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Judges 5:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:2:1)


**בִּפְרֹעַ פְּרָעוֹת בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל בְּהִתְנַדֵּב עָם.** כְּשֶׁבָּאוּ פְּרָצוֹת עַל יִשְׂרָאֵל שֶׁפָּרְצוּ בָּהֶם אוֹיְבֵיהֶם עַל עָזְבָם אֶת ה׳, וְהִתְנַדֵּב הָעָם לָשׁוּב בִּתְשׁוּבָה, מֵעַתָּה בָּרְכוּ אֶת ה׳ עַל הַתְּשׁוּעוֹת שֶׁעָשָׂה:


**When Yisroel was inundated with breaches.** When breaches befell Yisroel; when their enemies invaded them because they deserted Adonoy, and yet the people volunteered to repent—at this point, bless Adonoy for bringing deliverance.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on Judges 5:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:3:1)


**אָנֹכִי אָנֹכִי.** כָּפוּל, שָׁמַעְתִּי מִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה (ילקוט שמעוני ב מז) לֹא קִפֵּחַ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא שְׂכַר תָּבוֹר וְכַרְמֶל, שֶׁבָּאוּ לְמַתַּן תּוֹרָה לָתֵת עֲלֵיהֶם אֶת הַתּוֹרָה וְחָזְרוּ בְּבֹשֶׁת הַפָּנִים, אָמַר לָהֶם הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא, סוֹף שֶׁאֲנִי פּוֹרֵעַ לָכֶם כִּפְלַיִם, נֶאֱמַר בְּסִינַי (שמות כ:ב) אָנֹכִי ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ, בְּתָבוֹר יֵאָמַר: אָנֹכִי אָנֹכִי. נֶאֱמַר בְּסִינַי (שם) אָנֹכִי ה׳ אֱלֹהֶיךָ, בְּכַרְמֶל יֵאָמַר, כָּפוּל (מלכים א יח:לט) ה׳ הוּא הָאֱלֹהִים, ה׳ הוּא הָאֱלֹהִים, בִּימֵי אֵלִיָּהוּ:


**I…I.** The word is doubled. I have heard in the name of Midrash Agaddah: the Holy One blessed is He did not deny a reward for [Mount] Tovor and [Mount] Carmel, who came to the giving of Torah so that it might be given on them, and turned away in humiliation. The Holy One, blessed is He told them, "Eventually, I shall reward you doubly. At Sinai it is said, 'I am the Lord your God;'¹ at Tovor it shall be said, 'I…I.'² At Sinai it is said, 'I am the Lord your God;' At Carmel it shall be said doubly, 'Adonoy is God,' 'Adonoy is God!'³ in Eliyahu's days."⁴

footnotes:
¹ Shemos, 20:2.
² The word “I” is doubled.
³ Melachim 1, 18:39.
⁴ Yalkut, 47.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Judges 5:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:4:1)


**ה׳ בְּצֵאתְךָ מִשֵּׂעִיר.** זֶה מַתַּן תּוֹרָה, כְּמָה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (דברים לג:ב) וְזָרַח מִשֵּׂעִיר לָמוֹ, וּמַה עִנְיָנָהּ לְכָאן, כָּךְ אָמְרָה דְּבוֹרָה: קָשָׁה הִיא הַתּוֹרָה לִפְרֹשׁ מִמֶּנָּה וְטוֹבָה הִיא לִדְבַּק בָּהּ, שֶׁהֲרֵי בְּמוֹרָא וּבִגְבוּרָה נִתְּנָה, וּלְכָךְ נִמְסְרוּ יִשְׂרָאֵל בְּיַד אוֹיְבֵיהֶם עַל פְּרִישָׁתָם הֵימֶנָּה וּכְשֶׁהִתְנַדְּבוּ לַעֲסוֹק בָּהּ נוֹשְׁעוּ, כָּל זֶה יֵשׁ לִלְמֹד מִתַּרְגּוּמוֹ שֶׁל יוֹנָתָן:


**Adonoy, when You went forth from Seir,** This refers to the giving of Torah, as it is said, "…and shone forth from Seir to them."⁵ Why is this relevant here? This is what Devorah said: "The Torah is severe when forsaken, but rewarding when adhered to, as it was given in awesomeness and power.⁶ Thus, the Yisroelites were handed over to their enemies for forsaking it; and, when they offered to engross themselves in it, they were saved." All this we learn from Targum Yonasan's rendition.⁷

footnotes:
⁵ Devarim, 33:2. Adonoy first offered the Torah to Eisov’s descendants at Seir. When they refused, He shone forth from there to Yisroel at Sinai. (Rashi to Devarim, there).
⁶ As indicated by the quaking of the earth and the dripping of heaven in v.4, and the dissolving of the mountain in v.5.
⁷ “Adonoy, the Torah which you have given to Yisroel: When they violate it, their enemies dominate them, and when they return to it, they are empowered over their antagonists”. (Targum Yonasan)


###### Rashi on Judges 5:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:4:2)


**נָטְפוּ.** הֵזִילוּ טַל תְּחִיָּה:


**Dripped.** They dripped dew of revival.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ As the Yisroelites glowed with a heavenly fire, Adonoy instructed the clouds of glory to drip dew of revival to restore them. (Yalkut, 48)



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on Judges 5:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:5:1)


**הָרִים נָזְלוּ.** כְּמַיִם נוֹזְלִים נָמוֹגוּ:


**Mountains streamed.** Like streaming water—they dissolved.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:5:2)


**זֶה סִינַי.** כְּבָר מְפֹרָשׁ (שמות יט:כ) מַה נִרְאָה בּוֹ: עָשַׁן כֻּלּוֹ, וְקוֹל, וְלַפִּידִים וְאֵשׁ:


**This Sinai….** Scripture has already recounted the visions there—covered by mist, thunder, flashes of light, flame.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ Shemos, 19:18, 20:15, etc. Our passage, therefore, omits this, leaving it to the reader’s understanding. See the following Rashi.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:5:3)


**מִפְּנֵי ה׳.** וַהֲרֵי זֶה מִקְרָא קָצָר ׳זֶה סִינַי׳, מַה אֵרַע בּוֹ, מִפְּנֵי אֲשֶׁר יָרַד עָלָיו ה׳:


**Because of the presence of Adonoy** This is an shortened passage: "This Sinai—what took place there occured because the mountain was visited by Adonoy."



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on Judges 5:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:6:1)


**בִּימֵי שַׁמְגַּר בֶּן עֲנָת בִּימֵי יָעֵל.** מְלַמֵּד שֶׁאַף יָעֵל שָׁפְטָה אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל בְּיָמֶיהָ:


**In the days of Shamgar ben Anas, in the days of Yael.** This teaches that Yael, too, judged Yisroel in her days.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:6:2)


**חָדְלוּ אֳרָחוֹת.** הָיוּ יְרֵאִים יִשְׂרָאֵל לָצֵאת לְדַרְכָּם מִפְּנֵי הָאוֹיְבִים וְהָיוּ הוֹלְכִים דֶּרֶךְ עֲקַלָּתוֹן בְּהֵחָבֵא:


**Thoroughfares came to a halt.** The Yisroelites were afraid to undertake normal travel, because of their enemies. They would proceed along round about pathways, clandestinely.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on Judges 5:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:7:1)


**חָדְלוּ פְרָזוֹן.** עָרֵי הַפְּרָזִי שֶׁאֵין לָהֶם חוֹמָה חָדְלוּ מִהְיוֹת יוֹשְׁבִים בָּהֶם מִפְּנֵי אוֹיְבֵיהֶם, וְנֶאֶסְפוּ אֶל עָרֵי הַמִּבְצָר:


**Exposure was discontinued.** Exposed, unwalled cities were discontinued as dwelling places, because of the enemies, and they flocked to the fortified cities.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:7:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:7:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:7:2)


**עַד שַׁקַּמְתִּי.** כְּמוֹ שֶׁקַּמְתִּי, וְכֵן (שופטים ו:יז) שָׁאַתָּה מְדַבֵּר עִמִּי:


**Until I rose.** This¹⁰ vowelization for the *“segol”* in the opening ‘ש’ of שקמתי. The *“kametz”* vowelization of the opening ‘ש’ is substituted for the *“segol”.* is the same as שֶׁקַמְתִּי Similarly, 'That you are speaking to me'

footnotes:
¹⁰ Scripture substitutes the “pasach”



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on Judges 5:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:8:1)


**יִבְחַר אֱלֹהִים חֲדָשִׁים אָז לָחֶם שְׁעָרִים.** כְּשֶׁבָּחַר לוֹ יִשְׂרָאֵל אֱלֹהִים חֲדָשִׁים, אָז הֻזְקְקוּ לְמִלְחָמָה בְּשַׁעֲרֵיהֶם, רְאוּ עַתָּה בְּהִתְנַדְּבָם, אִם יֵרָאֶה בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל מָגֵן וָרֹמַח, שֶׁצָּרִיךְ לְהִלָּחֵם בְּאַרְבָּעִים אֶלֶף רָאשֵׁי גְיָסוֹת שֶׁהֵבִיא הַגּוֹי עֲלֵיהֶם, וְכֻלָּם הֲמָמָם הַמָּקוֹם בְּמִלְחֶמֶת הַכּוֹכָבִים (פסוק כא) וְנַחַל קִישׁוֹן גְּרָפָם:


**When they chose new gods—there was war in the cities.** When the Yisroelites preferred modern Deities, then they needed to engage in battle in their cities. But, take note, now, when they volunteered, was a shield or battle ax in sight among the Yisroelites when they needed to do battle with the forty thousand field marshals the idolators flung against them? The Omnipresent overwhelmed them all with celestial warfare, and the Kishon stream swept them away.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:8:2)


**שְׁעָרִים.** כְּמוֹ (דברים יז:ב) כִּי יִמָּצֵא בְקִרְבְּךָ בְּאַחַד שְׁעָרֶיךָ, עֲיָירוֹת:


**Cities.** As in, "If there should be found in your midst, in one of your cities".¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ Devarim, 17:2. Although generally translated as “gates”, שְׁעָרִים is translated here as “cities”.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on Judges 5:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:9:1)


**לִבִּי.** אֲנִי דְּבוֹרָה לְחוֹקְקֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל, לֶאֱהֹב אֶת חַכְמֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל שֶׁהִתְנַדְּבוּ בָּעָם, לֵאמֹר: בָּרְכוּ אֶת ה׳ וְשׁוּבוּ אֵלָיו:


**My heart.** I, Devorah, [my heart is out] to the legislators of Yisroel—with affection for the sages of Yisroel who volunteered, among the people, saying, "Bless Adonoy, return to Him".



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on Judges 5:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:10:1)


**רֹכְבֵי אֲתֹנוֹת צְחֹרוֹת.** הַסּוֹחֲרִים וְהַשָּׂרִים שֶׁיִּרְכְּבוּ מֵעַתָּה עַל אֲתוֹנוֹת לְבָנוֹת הַחֲשׁוּבוֹת, מִבְּלִי יִרְאָה וְדַאֲגַת אוֹיְבֵיהֶם, וְאוֹתָן שֶׁיּוֹשְׁבִין עַל מִדִּין, לְשׁוֹן דַּיָּנִים, שֶׁהָיוּ יְרֵאִים לַעֲשׂוֹת מִשְׁפָּט בְּפַרְהֶסְיָא, וְכֵן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: דַּהֲווֹ מִתְחַבְּרִין לְמֵיתַב עַל דִּינָא, וְאוֹתָן שֶׁהוֹלְכִין רַגְלִי עַל דֶּרֶךְ, שִׂיחוּ וְסַפְּרוּ אֶת הַתְּשׁוּעָה הַזֹּאת, אֲשֶׁר מִקּוֹל הַמְחַצְּצִים אֶתְכֶם, אוֹרְבִים, לִסְטִים וּמוֹכְסִין, הַיּוֹשְׁבִין גְּדוּדִים בְּמַעֲרָכוֹת לֶאֱרֹב בְּעָבְרֵי הַמַּיִם, שָׁם יִתְּנוּ עַתָּה רוֹכְבֵי הָאֲתוֹנוֹת וְהוֹלְכֵי עַל דֶּרֶךְ אֶת צִדְקוֹת ה׳, וּמַה הֵן הַצְּדָקוֹת (פסוק יא) צִדְקוֹת פִּרְזוֹנוֹ בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל שֶׁהִשְׁקִיט אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל, לִשְׁכּוֹן פְּרָזוֹן וְלָצֵאת מְפֻזָּרִים בִּיחִידִי, שֶׁלֹּא בַּאֲסִיפַת עָם:


**Riders of white donkeys.** Merchants and gentry who may now ride elegant, white donkeys without intimidation or concern about their enemies. And jurists—this refers to the magistrates who were afraid to deliver judgement publicly, as Targum Yonasan's translates, "Those who assembled to sit in judgement". And wayfarers on foot. Speak up! Recount this salvation, when, in place of the clamor of the formations battling you—marauders, thieves, tax processors, who station themselves in battle formations organized to waylay those crossing to watering places—there, the donkey riders and wayfarers will retell Adonoy's charity. What is this charity? The charity of His restoration of exposure in Yisroel, when He brought tranquility to Yisroel, allowing them to dwell in exposed cities, and to venture forth when dipersed,¹² singly, without grouping together. Then, they went down to the cities, returning to the cities they had inhabited when they fled to congregate in the fortified cities.

footnotes:
¹² The Talmud (Pesachim, 87:b) reads פּֽרָזוֹן, lit. “exposure”, as פִּזְרוֹן, “dispersion”. Rashi follows this interpretation.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on Judges 5:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:11:1)


**מְחַצְצִים.** פָּתַר מְנַחֵם: עוֹרְכֵי מִלְחָמָה וְסוֹדְרֵי קְרָב, וְחִבְּרוֹ עִם (משלי ל:כז) מֶלֶךְ אֵין לָאַרְבֶּה וַיֵּצֵא חֹצֵץ כֻּלּוֹ, לְשׁוֹן גְּדוּדִים. דָּבָר אַחֵר:


**Battle formations.** Menachem interprets "strategists, military tactitians."¹³ He relates this to "The locust has no king, yet he advances entirely 'in formation'."¹⁴ The meaning is 'battle formations." Another interpretation,¹⁵

footnotes:
¹³ Meaning “the battle formations organized by strategists and tactitians.”
¹⁴ Mishlei, 30:27.
¹⁵ Both interpretations appear in Targum Yonasan.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:11:2)


**מִקּוֹל מְחַצְצִים בֵּין מַשְׁאַבִּים.** כְּשֶׁהֱיִיתֶם הוֹלְכִים בֵּין מַשְׁאַבִּים לִשְׁאֹב מַיִם, הֱיִיתֶם יְרֵאִים מִקּוֹל אֲבָנִים, חַלּוּקֵי הַנַּחַל, קיזל״ש בְּלַעַ״ז, שֶׁהָעוֹבֵר עֲלֵיהֶם נִשְׁמָע קוֹלָם, וֶהֱיִיתֶם יְרֵאִים שֶׁלֹּא יַרְגִּישׁוּ בָכֶם אוֹיְבֵיכֶם, מֵעַכְשָׁיו אֵין לָחוּשׁ עַל זֹאת, וּמִשּׁוּם הָכִי מִצְוָה עֲלֵיכֶם לִיתֵּן הוֹדָיָה לִשְׁמוֹ:


**Concerning the clatter of pebbles amidst the watering places.** When you would walk amidst the watering places to draw water, you were terrified by the clatter of the pebbles smoothed by the stream, "cailloux" in old French. When stepped on, they emit a noise, and you were afraid that your presence would be sensed by your enemies. Now, however, such fears are baseless, and this is why you are commanded to offer thanks to His Name.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:11:3)


**מְחַצְצִים.** לְשׁוֹן אַבְנֵי חִלּוּקֵי הַנַּחַל, כְּמוֹ (איכה ג:טז): ״וַיַּגְרֵס בֶּחָצָץ״:


**Pebbles.** This refers to pebbles smoothed by a stream, as in "He ground with pebbles."¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁶ Eichah, 3:16.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:11:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:11:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:11:4)


**אָז יָרְדוּ לַשְּׁעָרִים.** חָזְרוּ לְעָרֵי מוֹשְׁבוֹתָם שֶׁבָּרְחוּ מֵהֶם לֵיאָסֵף אֶל עָרֵי הַמִּבְצָר:



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Judges 5:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:12:1)


**עוּרִי עוּרִי דְּבוֹרָה.** לְשׁוֹן שֶׁבַח כִּפְשׁוּטוֹ, הִתְגַּבְּרִי בְּשִׁירָתֵךְ וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ (פסחים סו:) מִפְּנֵי שֶׁנִּתְהַלְּלָה וְאָמְרָה (פסוק ז) עַד שַׁקַּמְתִּי דְּבוֹרָה, נִסְתַּלְּקָה הֵימֶנָּה רוּחַ הַקֹּדֶשׁ:


**Awaken, awaken.** The inference is, "awaken to praise," as plainly indicated. "Intensify your singing." However, the Rabbis remark,¹⁷ because she was self lauditory and said, "Until I rose, Devorah,"¹⁸ the Divine spirit departed from her.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁷ Pesachim, 66:b.
¹⁸ Thereby favoring herself over the leaders who preceded her. (Rashi to Pesachim, ibid.)
¹⁹ Silencing her song (Rashi to Pesachim, ibid). It was now necessary for her to reinvoke the prophetic spirit by saying, “Awake, awake.”



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Judges 5:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:13:1)


**אָז יָרַד שָׂרִיד לְאַדִּירִים.** יָרַד - יִרְדֶּה, כְּמוֹ (ישעיהו מה:א) לְרַד לְפָנָיו גוֹיִם, שְׂרִידֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל רָדוּ בְּאַדִּירֵי הַגּוֹיִם:


**Dominated.**יְרַד is similar to יִרְדֶה, "dominated,"²⁰ as in "to prostrate nations before him."²¹ The surviving Yisroelites dominated the mighty among the idolators.

footnotes:
²⁰ יָרַד is translated as descended. יְרַד, however, means dominated.
²¹ To give him dominion over them. (Yeshayah, 45:1.)


###### Rashi on Judges 5:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:13:2)


**ה׳ יְרַד לִי בַּגִּבּוֹרִים.** יִרְדֶּה לִי אֶת גִּבּוֹרֵי הַגּוֹיִם:


**Adonoy empowered me over the mighty.** Gave me dominion²² over the mighty warriors among the nations.

footnotes:
²² See fn.19.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on Judges 5:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:14:1)


**מִנִּי אֶפְרַיִם.** מִן אֶפְרַיִם יָצָא שׁוֹרֶשׁ יְהוֹשֻׁעַ בֶּן נוּן, לִרְדּוֹת בַּעֲמָלֵק לְחַלָּשׁ אוֹתוֹ לְפִי חֶרֶב, וּמְחֻבָּר מִקְרָא זֶה לְעֶלְיוֹן, לְפָרֵשׁ: ה׳ יְרַד לִי בַּגִּבּוֹרִים, אֶת יְהוֹשֻׁעַ הֵקִים לְרַד בַּעֲמָלֵק:


**In Ephraim.** From Ephraim emerged the root of Yehoshua bin Nun's dominance over Amaleik²³ when he debilitated them with the sword.²⁴ This passage is related to the preceding one, as it explains "Adonoy empowered me over the mighty" by establishing Yehoshua's dominance over Amaleik.

footnotes:
²³ Yehoshua was descended from Ephraim, son of Yoseif, and Amaleik was a descendant of Eisov. Scripture states, “The house of Yaakov shall be fire, the house of Yoseif flame, the house of Eisov chaff” [Ovadiah, 18], teaching that the descendants of Yoseif shall devour the descendants of Eisov as flame consumes chaff. (Yalkut, 51. Cf. Rashi to Bereishis, 30:25)
²⁴ Shemos, 17:13.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:14:2)


**אַחֲרֶיךָ.** יָקוּם מִשֵּׁבֶט בִּנְיָמִין שָׁאוּל בֶּן קִישׁ שֶׁיִּגְרְמֶנּוּ וִיעַמֵּם אוֹתוֹ כְּגֶחָלִים עוֹמְמוֹת וְיֵשׁ לִפְתּוֹר: ״בַּעֲמָמֶיךָ״, בְּחַיִל שֶׁל מָאתַיִם אֶלֶף רַגְלִי שֶׁבָּא שָׁאוּל עֲלֵיהֶם:


**Your successor** shall arise from the tribe of Binyomin.²⁵ Shaul ben Kish, who will batter them²⁶ and extinguish them like flickering embers. Another interpretation: "with your people,²⁷ referring to the army of two hundred thousand infantrymen which Shaul mobilized against them.²⁸

footnotes:
²⁵ Like Yoseif, Binyomin was a child of Rochel, and Eisov is destined to fall by the power of Rochel’s descendants. (Yalkut, ibid.)
²⁶ Shmuel 1, 15:7. This took place during the time of the prophet Shmuel, after the days of Devorah and the Shoftim. Her remarks here are prophetic. (Radak)
²⁷ “Your succesor, with your people.”
²⁸ I Shmuel 15:4.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:14:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:14:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:14:3)


**מִנִּי מָכִיר יָרְדוּ מְחֹקְקִים.** שָׂרִים גְּדוֹלִים שֶׁכָּבְשׁוּ אֶת הָאֱמוֹרִי, אֲשֶׁר כָּבְשׁוּ (דברים ג:ד) שִׁשִּׁים עִיר כָּל חֶבֶל אַרְגּוֹב שֶׁכָּבַשׁ יָאִיר:


**From Mochir descended warlords.** Great lords who vanquished the Emorites. They captured sixty cities—the entire imperial zone conquered by Yair.²⁹

footnotes:
²⁹ Ben Menashe. (Bemidbar, 32:41, Devarim, 3:14).



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Judges 5:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:15:1)


**וְשָׂרַי בְּיִשָּׂשכָר.** שָׂרִים שֶׁבְּיִשָּׂשכָר, הֵם סַנְהֶדְרָאוֹת עוֹסְקִין בַּתּוֹרָה, (דברי הימים א יב:לג) יוֹדְעֵי בִינָה לָעִתִּים, הֵם תָּמִיד עִם דְּבוֹרָה לְלַמֵּד בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל חֹק וּמִשְׁפָּט:


**Yesochar's nobles.** The noblemen of Yesochar. This refers to members of the Sanhedrin who were engrossed in Torah study, and who knew and understood the times.³⁰ They constantly accompanied Devorah, teaching the Yisroelites the laws and precepts.

footnotes:
³⁰ Divrei Hayamim (1, 12:32) lists, among King Davids followers, “Of the sons of Yesachar, who knew and understood the times.” They understood the nature of the events taking place in their times, and were, therefore, qualified to serve as King David’s advisors. (Rashi to Divrei Hayamim, there). Or, they were expertly versed in the calculations used in fixing the months and years of the Jewish calendar. They knew and understood the determination of the times of the calendar. (Yalkut, here, 52. See Rashi to Bereishis, 49:15)


###### Rashi on Judges 5:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:15:2)


**וְשָׂרַי בְּיִשָּׂשכָר.** יוּ״ד זוֹ טְפֵלָה הִיא וְאֵינָהּ מְשַׁמֶּשֶׁת כְּלוּם, וַהֲרֵי הִיא כְּיוּ״ד (תהלים ח:ח) בַּהֲמוֹת שָׂדַי, (ירמיהו כב:יד) וְקָרַע לוֹ חַלּוֹנָי:


**Yesochar's nobles.** The "י" in שׇׂרַי [lit. "my" nobles] is ancillary, fulfilling no grammatic function. It is similar to the "י" in "the animals in the field,"³¹ or "he cut open, for himself, a window."³²

footnotes:
³¹ Tehilim, 8:8. שׇׂדַי, lit. “my” field, is translated as שׇׂדֶה, “the field.”
³² Yermiyahu, 22:14. חַלּוֹנַי, lit. “my windows,” is translated as חַלּוֹן, “the window.”


###### Rashi on Judges 5:15:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:15:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:15:3)


**וְיִשָּׂשכָר כֵּן בָּרָק.** וּשְׁאָר הָעָם שֶׁל יִשָּׂשכָר, כֵּן הֵם עִם בָּרָק לְכָל אֲשֶׁר יְצַוֶּה:


**Yesochar with Barak.** The remainder of Yesochar's constituency were likewise at Barak's disposal whenever he issued orders.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:15:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:15:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:15:4)


**בָּעֵמֶק שֻׁלַּח בְּרַגְלָיו.** שְׁלָחָם בְּכָל שְׁלִיחוּתוֹ, וְלֶאֱסוֹף אֶת הָעָם וּלְכָל צָרְכֵי הַמִּלְחָמָה:


**Dispatched to the valley at his heels.** He dispatched them on all of his missions—to mobilize the people, and for all military needs.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:15:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:15:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:15:5)


**בִּפְלַגּוֹת רְאוּבֵן.** אֲבָל בְּחִלּוּקֵי לִבּוֹ שֶׁל רְאוּבֵן רַבּוּ **חִקְקֵי לֵב.** (תרגום:) נִכְלֵי לִיבָּא, עָרְמוּמִית, וּמָה הִיא עָרְמוּמִיתוֹ, יָשַׁב לוֹ בֵּין מִשְׁפְּתֵי הַמִּלְחָמָה לִשְׁמוֹעַ מִי נוֹצֵחַ וְיִהְיֶה עִמּוֹ:


**However, in Reuven's equivocation.** However, in Reuven's ambiguity was great cunning. "Craftiness of heart,"³³ cunning. In what way was he cunning? He straddled the battle lines to learn who would emerge the victor so that he could join them.

footnotes:
³³ This is Targum Yonasan’s translation.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on Judges 5:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:16:1)


**שְׁרִיקוֹת עֲדָרִים.** לִשְׁמוֹעַ קוֹל עֶדְרֵי הַמִּלְחָמָה, לְמִי קוֹל עֲנוֹת גְּבוּרָה וּלְמִי קוֹל עֲנוֹת חֲלוּשָׁה:


**The shrieking companies.** Listening to the sounds emitted by the companies engaged in battle, to determine which was the cry of triumph, and which the cry of defeat.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on Judges 5:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:17:1)


**בְּעֵבֶר הַיַּרְדֵּן שָׁכֵן.** וְלֹא בָא אֶל הַמִּלְחָמָה, וְכֵן דָּן הִכְנִיס מָמוֹנוֹ בִּסְפִינוֹת לַעֲמוֹד וְלִבְרוֹחַ:


**Is ensconced beyond the Yardein** and never arrived at the battle front. Similarly, Dan hoarded his money into sea vessels, poised to escape.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:17:2)


**וְעַל מִפְרָצָיו.** לִשְׁמוֹר פִּרְצוֹת אַרְצוֹ:


**At his exposures.** To defend his exposed territory.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on Judges 5:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:18:1)


**זְבֻלוּן עַם חֵרֵף וְגוֹ׳.** בִּזָּה נַפְשׁוֹ וְהִפְקִיר עַצְמוֹ לָמוּת בַּמִּלְחָמָה עִם בָּרָק, וְכֵן נַפְתָּלִי עַל מְרוֹמֵי שָׂדֶה עַל הַר תָּבוֹר:


**Zevulun; a people who disdained, etc.** They were contemptuous of their lives, and subjected themselves to death in battle with Barak. Likewise, Naftoli at the highland field on Mount Tovor.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on Judges 5:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:19:1)


**בְּתַעֲנַךְ עַל מֵי מְגִדּוֹ.** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: בְּתַעֲנַךְ הֲווֹ שָׁרָן וּמָטָן עַל מֵי מְגִדּוֹ, רֹאשׁ הַמַּחֲנֶה בְּתַעֲנַךְ וּמַגִּיעַ עַד מֵי מְגִדּוֹ:


**At Ta'anach to the waters of Megiddo.** Targum Yonasan renders, "They were encamped at Ta'anach, and reached the waters of Megiddo." The upper section of the camp was at Ta'anach, and it extended to the waters of Megiddo.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:19:2)


**בֶּצַע כֶּסֶף לֹא לָקָחוּ.** חִנָּם בָּאוּ לְעֶזְרַת סִיסְרָא, לֹא בִּקְשׁוּ מִמֶּנּוּ שָׂכָר:


**Deigning no mercenary reward.** They came to Sisera's assistance gratuitously—without requesting payment.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on Judges 5:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:20:1)


**מִן שָׁמַיִם נִלְחָמוּ.** וְאַף הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא שָׁלַח בָּם צְבָאָיו שֶׁאֵינָם רוֹצִים שׁוּם שָׂכָר:


**War was waged from heaven.** The Holy One blessed is He, as well, dispatched His hosts against them. They, too, have no desire for any payment.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:20:2)


**מִמְּסִלּוֹתָם.** רֹאשׁ הַכּוֹכָב בַּשָּׁמַיִם וְסוֹפוֹ בָּאָרֶץ, מִכָּאן אָמְרוּ (תנחומא בראשית ה) עָבְיוֹ שֶׁל רָקִיעַ כַּאֲוִיר שֶׁבֵּין שָׁמַיִם לָאָרֶץ, שֶׁהַכּוֹכָב תָּקוּעַ בָּרָקִיעַ כְּמִין נֶגֶר בְּדֶלֶת, מִדַּת אָרְכּוֹ כְּעָבְיוֹ שֶׁל רָקִיעַ, וּמִן הַמִּקְרָא שֶׁמֵּעִיד ׳מִמְּסִלּוֹתָם נִלְחָמוּ׳, אָנוּ שׁוֹמְעִין שֶׁעָבְיוֹ שֶׁל רָקִיעַ כְּגוֹבַהּ שָׁמַיִם מִן הָאָרֶץ:


**In their pathways.** The upper tip of the star was in heaven, the bottom on earth. This teaches that the width of the firmament is equivalent to the distance between heaven and earth. For the star is suspended across the firmament like a sort of bolt across a door, its length measuring the same as the width of the firmament. From this passage, which attests that they waged war in their pathways, we learn that the width of the firmament is the same as the elevation of heaven from earth.³⁴

footnotes:
³⁴ “In their pathways” indicates that the star remained in its celestial path during the battle on earth. Evidently, the tip of the star remained fixed in heaven, while the remainder swung downward so that the lower edge reached the earth and destroyed Sisera’s forces. Since the star now extended from heaven to earth normally reached across the firmament, we learn that the distance between the two areas is the same.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on Judges 5:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:21:1)


**גְּרָפָם.** טֵאטֵם מִן הָעוֹלָם, כְּמַגְרֵפָה זוֹ שֶׁגּוֹרֶפֶת אֶת הָאֵפֶר מִן הַכִּירָה:


**Swept them away.** It brushed them from the world like a broom which brushes ashes from an oven.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:21:2)


**נַחַל קְדוּמִים.** שֶׁנַּעֲשָׂה עָרֵב לַיָּם עַל זֶה מִימוֹת מִצְרַיִם, כִּדְאִיתָא בִּפְסָחִים (קיח:):


**The stream of antiquity.** It guaranteed the obligation to the sea concerning this, from the days of Egypt,³⁵ as stated in Pesachim.³⁶

footnotes:
³⁵ In the days of antiquity, during the exodus from Egypt.
³⁶ 118:b. When the Egyptians drowned at the splitting of the Reed Sea, Adonoy ordered the sea to cast their bodies onto the shore, to prove to the Yisroelites that they had perished. The sea objected, claiming the bodies as its due, and consented only when Kishon stream guaranteed repayment of the obligation. Kishon now fulfilled the guarantee, by sweeping Sisera’s forces into the sea.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:21:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:21:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:21:3)


**תִּדְרְכִי נַפְשִׁי.** בָּרַגְלַיִם אֶת עֹז גִּבּוֹרֵי כְנָעַן:


**My soul trampled underfoot** the power of the mighty Canaanite warriors.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on Judges 5:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:22:1)


**הָלְמוּ עִקְּבֵי סוּס.** נִשְׁתַּלְּפוּ טַלְפֵי סוּסֵיהֶם, שֶׁהָיָה חֹם הַכּוֹכָב מַרְתִּיחַ אֶת הַטִּיט וְהַצִּפּוֹרֶן נִשְׁמְטָה, כְּמוֹ שֶׁמּוֹלְגִין אֶת הָרַגְלַיִם בְּרוֹתְחִין וְהַטְּלָפִים נִשְׁמָטִים, מִתּוֹךְ שֶׁנִּשְׁתַּלְּפוּ נֶהֶלְמוּ הָרַגְלַיִם:


**Horses' legs were battered.** Their horses' hooves were severed. The heat of the star brought the mud to a boil, severing the hooves, as when legs are scalded in boiling water to detach the hooves. Once the hooves were severed, the legs were battered.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:22:2)


**מִדַּהֲרוֹת דַּהֲרוֹת אַבִּירָיו.** שֶׁהָיוּ מַרְקִידִים אֶת סוּסֵיהֶם בַּמִּלְחָמָה, כְּמוֹ (נחום ג:ב) וְסוּס דֹּהֵר, הוּא לְשׁוֹן הַרְקָדַת הַסּוּס:


**By the prancing, the prancing of their mighty.** They pranced their horses in battle, as in "a prancing horse.³⁷ The word connotes the dancing of a horse.

footnotes:
³⁷ Nachum, 3:2.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on Judges 5:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:23:1)


**אוֹרוּ מֵרוֹז.** אָמְרֵי לַהּ כּוֹכְבָא הֲוָה, וְאָמְרֵי לַהּ גַּבְרָא חֲשִׁיבָא הֲוָה (מועד קטן טז.), וְהָיָה סָמוּךְ לִמְקוֹם הַמִּלְחָמָה וְלֹא בָא:
