**Curse Meroz!** One opinion is that this was a star.³⁸ According to others, this was a distinguished person who was near the battle field but failed to appear.³⁹

footnotes:
³⁸ Sisera’s protective star. (Rashi to Moed Katan, 16:a).
³⁹ Moed Katan ibid.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:23:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:23:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:23:2)


**אָמַר מַלְאַךְ ה׳.** אָמַר בָּרָק בִּשְׁלִיחוּתוֹ שֶׁל הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא:


**Declares Adonoy's emissary.** Barak made this declaration, as the emissary of the Holy One, blessed is He.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:23:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:23:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:23:3)


**יוֹשְׁבֶיהָ.** הַיּוֹשְׁבִים בְּאַרְבַּע אַמּוֹת שֶׁלּוֹ (שם) בְּאַרְבַּע מְאָה שִׁיפּוּרֵי שַׁמְּתֵיהּ בָּרָק לְמֵרוֹז:


**Those who dwell in it.** Those who remain within his four *amohs.* With four hundred shofar blasts, Barak excommunicated Meroz.⁴⁰

footnotes:
⁴⁰ Ibid.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:23:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:23:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:23:4)


**לְעֶזְרַת ה׳.** כִּבְיָכוֹל, שֶׁמִּי שֶׁהוּא עוֹזֵר אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל, כְּעוֹזֵר אֶת הַשְּׁכִינָה:


**To assist Adonoy.** As if it were possible,⁴¹ if anyone comes to Yisroel's assistance, it is as if he were assisting the Divine Presence.⁴²

footnotes:
⁴¹ כביכול, “as if it were possible,” is used as a caveat when human qualities seem to be attributed to Adonoy.
⁴² Yalkut, 55.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on Judges 5:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:24:1)


**מִנָּשִׁים בָּאֹהֶל.** שָׂרָה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בָּהּ (בראשית יח:ט) הִנֵּה בָאֹהֶל. רִבְקָה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בָּהּ (בראשית כד:סז) וַיְבִיאֶהָ יִצְחָק הָאֹהֱלָה. רָחֵל וְלֵאָה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בָּהֶן (בראשית לא:לג) וַיֵּצֵא מֵאֹהֶל לֵאָה וְגוֹ׳:


**By the women of the tent.** Sarah, of whom it is said, "Here she is, in the tent."⁴³ Rivkah, of whom it is said, "Yitzchak brought her to the tent."⁴⁴ Rachel and Leah, of whom it is said, "He left Leah's tent and entered Rachel's tent."⁴⁵

footnotes:
⁴³ Bereishis, 18:9.
⁴⁴ Ibid, 24:67.
⁴⁵ Ibid, 31:33.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:24:2)


**מִנָּשִׁים בָּאֹהֶל תְּבֹרָךְ.** יָעֵל לָמָּה - הֵן יָלְדוּ וְגִדְּלוּ, וְאִם לֹא יָעֵל הֲרֵי הָרָשָׁע הַזֶּה בָּא וּמְאַבְּדָן, כָּךְ הוּא בִּבְרֵאשִׁית רַבָּה (מח:טו). דָּבָר אַחֵר, אַף יָעֵל הָיְתָה יוֹשֶׁבֶת אֹהָלִים, לְפִיכָךְ הִזְכִּיר אוֹתָהּ בְּבִרְכַּת אֹהָלִים:


**Blessed by the women of the tent.** Yael was blessed. Why? They bore and raised children. Were it not for Yael, this scoundrel would have annihilated them. This is taught in Breishis Rabbah.⁴⁶ Another interpretation: Yael, as well, remained in the tent,⁴⁷ and she is therefore, mentioned in the blessing of the women of the tents.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on Judges 5:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:25:1)


**חָלָב נָתָנָה.** לָדַעַת אִם לִבּוֹ עָלָיו שֶׁיָּבִין בֵּין טַעַם מַיִם לְטַעַם חָלָב, כֵּן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן:


**But she gave milk** to ascertain whether he was alert enough to differentiate between the taste of water and the taste of milk. This is Yonasan's rendition.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:25:2)


**בְּסֵפֶל אַדִּירִים.** בְּסֵפֶל שֶׁשּׁוֹתִין בּוֹ מַיִם, שֶׁהַמַּיִם נִקְרְאוּ ׳אַדִּירִים׳, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (שמות טו:י): בְּמַיִם אַדִּירִים:


**In a cup of the mighty.** In a cup used for drinking water, for waters are described as "mighty", as it is said, "in mighty waters."⁴⁸

footnotes:
⁴⁸ Shemos, 15:10.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on Judges 5:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:26:1)


**לְהַלְמוּת עֲמֵלִים.** סִיסְרָא, שֶׁהָיָה עָמֵל וְיָגֵעַ:


**To batter the exhausted** Sisera, who was exhausted, totally fatigued.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:26:2)


**מָחֲקָה.** לְשׁוֹן חִיקּוּי:


**Penetrated.** From חִיקּוּי, "penetration".


###### Rashi on Judges 5:26:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:26:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:26:3)


**וּמָחֲצָה.** לְשׁוֹן מַחַץ:


**Inflicted grievous injury.** From מַחַץ, "injury".


###### Rashi on Judges 5:26:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:26:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:26:4)


**וְחָלְפָה.** יוֹצֵא מִצַּד אַחֵר:


**Impaled.** It emerged from the other side.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on Judges 5:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:28:1)


**וַתְּיַבֵּב.** לְשׁוֹן דִּבּוּר, כְּמוֹ (ישעיהו נז:יט) נִיב שְׂפָתַיִם וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ פֵּרְשׁוּהוּ (ראש השנה לג:) לְשׁוֹן גְּנִיחָה, כִּתְרוּעָה, דִּמְתַרְגְּמִינָן ׳יְבָבָא׳ (ויקרא כג:כד). וַאֲנִי אוֹמֵר: לְשׁוֹן רְאִיָּה, כְּמוֹ (זכריה ב:ב) בָּבַת עֵינִי, וְכֵן חִבְּרוֹ מְנַחֵם:


**Moaning.** The word refers to expressing speech, as in "the expression of the lips."⁴⁹ However, the Rabbis⁵⁰ interpret this as referring to moaning, as in the shofar's *"teruah"* sound, translated יַבָּבָא, "moan".⁵¹ In my opinion, the reference is to seeing, as in "the pupil of his eye."⁵² This is Menachem's interpretation.

footnotes:
⁴⁹ Yeshaya, 57:19.
⁵⁰ Rosh Hashona 33:b.
⁵¹ Bemidbar, 29:1.
⁵² Zechariah, 2:12.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:28:2)


**אֶשְׁנָב.** חַלּוֹן:


A window.



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on Judges 5:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:29:1)


**חַכְמוֹת שָׂרוֹתֶיהָ תַּעֲנֶינָה.** הַחֲכָמוֹת שֶׁבְּשָׂרוֹתֶיהָ, לְכָךְ נָקוּד חֵי״ת פַּתַּ״ח, חָכְמוֹת שֶׁהוּא שֵׁם דָּבָר נָקוּד חֲטַ״ף קָמַ״ץ, כְּמוֹ (משלי יד:א) רָאמוֹת לֶאֱוִיל חָכְמוֹת, וְזֶה נָקוּד חַכְמוֹת, וְכֵן (שם יד:א) חַכְמוֹת נָשִׁים בָּנְתָה בֵיתָהּ, חַכְמוֹת שֶׁבַּנָּשִׁים:


**The wise among her ladies responded to her.** The wise ones⁵³ among her ladies. This is why the "ח" is vocalized with a *"pasach".* The plural noun, lit. "wisdoms" is vocalized with the half-*kametz*, as in "Wisdoms are remote gems to the foolish."⁵⁴ Here, the vocalization is חַכְמוֹת. Similarly, "The wise among women has constructed her home."⁵⁵ The wise one among the women.

footnotes:
⁵³ Not “the wisdoms of her ladies.” The vocalization indicates that the proper translation is “the wise ones,” rather than “the wisdoms,” as Rashi explains.
⁵⁴ Mishlei, 24:7.
⁵⁵ Mishlei, 14:1.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:29:2)


**תַּעֲנֶינָּה.** דַּגְשׁוּת שֶׁל נוּ״ן מְשַׁמֶּשֶׁת בִּמְקוֹם נוּ״ן שְׁלִישִׁית, וּפִתְרוֹנוֹ תַּעֲנֶינָּה אוֹתָהּ, דַּגְשׁוּת נוּ״ן מְשַׁמֶּשֶׁת בִּמְקוֹם ׳אוֹתָהּ׳, כְּמוֹ (ויקרא ו:יד) מֻרְבֶּכֶת תְּבִיאֶנָּה:


**Responded.** The period punctuating the נ serves as a substitute for a third נ.⁵⁶ Hence, the translation is "[the wise ones] responded 'to her.'" The period punctuating נ serves as a substitute for "her",⁵⁷ as in "you shall bring 'her' scalded."⁵⁸

footnotes:
⁵⁶ The third person feminine plural for “response” is תַּעֲנׇנׇה, “they responded”. Also, as a general rule, the suffix נה is added as a contraction of אוֹתׇהּ, “to her.” Thus, “they responded to her” should actually read וַתַּעֲנׇנׇה, with the נ tripled. However, Rashi notes, Scripture drops the third נ, and punctuates the second נ with a period to represent the missing letter.
⁵⁷ A punctuated נ may appear where no letter has been dropped. Then, its purpose is to represent a contraction of אוֹתהּ, “her”.
⁵⁸ Vayikra, 6:14. This refers to the feminine מִנְחָה.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:29:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:29:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:29:3)


**אַף הִיא תָּשִׁיב אֲמָרֶיהָ.** תַּנְחוּמִין לְעַצְמָהּ: מָה אֲנִי מִתְמַהּ תְּמִיהָ עַל עִכּוּב בְּנִי:


**Indeed, she stated in reply.** She consoled herself. Why should I keep wondering about my son's delay?



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on Judges 5:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:30:1)


**הֲלֹא יִמְצְאוּ יְחַלְּקוּ שָׁלָל.** וְהוּא מְעַכְּבָן:


**Are they not finding** and dividing the pillage? That is the cause of the delay.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:30:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:30:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:30:2)


**רַחֲמָתַיִם לְרֹאשׁ גֶּבֶר.** מְעַנִּים נָשִׁים יָפוֹת בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל, וְכָל אִישׁ מֵהֶם יֵשׁ לוֹ בְּמִטָּתוֹ שְׁתַּיִם וְשָׁלֹשׁ נָשִׁים:


**Or two [wombs] for each man.** They are ravishing the attractive Yisroelite women, and each of them has, in his bed, two or three women.



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on Judges 5:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:31:1)


**כֵּן יֹאבְדוּ.** דְּבוֹרָה אָמְרָה: תַּנְחוּמִין שֶׁל הֶבֶל הֵן לָהּ, כֵּן יֹאבְדוּ כָל אוֹיְבֶיךָ ה׳, כַּאֲשֶׁר אָבַד הוּא:


**So may perish**. Devorah declared,⁵⁹ her consolation is hollow—may all your enemies perish, Adonoy, as he perished.

footnotes:
⁵⁹ Our verse was not recited by Sisera’s mother, but by Devorah. (Yalkut, 58.)


###### Rashi on Judges 5:31:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:31:2)


**וְאֹהֲבָיו כְּצֵאת הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ בִּגְבוּרָתוֹ.** לֶעָתִיד לָבֹא, (ישעיהו ל:כו) שִׁבְעָתַיִם כְּאוֹר שִׁבְעַת הַיָּמִים, שֶׁהִיא עַל אַחַת שְׁלֹשׁ מֵאוֹת וְאַרְבָּעִים וְשָׁלֹשׁ כְּמִסְפַּר אַרְבָּעִים וְתֵשַׁע שְׁבִיעִיּוֹת:


**But those who love him**—as the sun emerging in its full power in the world to come, sevenfold over the light of the seven days of creation, or three hundred forty three to one. This equals forty nine times seven.⁶⁰

footnotes:
⁶⁰ Sevenfold over the light of all seven days of creation is equivalent to forty nine-fold over the original light of creation. (Rashi to Zechariah, 4:3). The light of creation was sevenfold over our present light. (Tosafos to Bava Basra, 8:b). Hence, the light of the world to come will be one hundred forty three times as great as our present light.


###### Rashi on Judges 5:31:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:31:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/5:31:3)


**וַתִּשְׁקֹט הָאָרֶץ.** אֵין זֶה מִדִּבְרֵי דְבוֹרָה אֶלָּא מִדִּבְרֵי כּוֹתֵב הַסֵּפֶר:


**The land was tranquil for forty years.** These words are not Devorah's, but the author's.



## Chapter 6


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Judges 6:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:1:1)


**וַיַּעֲשׂוּ הָרַע.** עַד כָּאן נֶאֱמַר ׳וַיּוֹסִיפוּ׳, שֶׁהָיָה נִצְבָּר חֵטְא עַל חֵטְא, אֲבָל בְּשִׁירָה זוֹ נִמְחַל לָהֶם כָּל אֲשֶׁר עָשׂוּ, וְעַכְשָׁיו מַתְחִילִין לַחֲטוֹא, כָּךְ נִדְרַשׁ בְּאַגָּדַת תְּהִלִּים (מדרש תהלים יח ו):


**Acted wickedly.** Until this period, Scripture states, "They resumed [acting wickedly]" since their iniquities accumulated. However, through this hymn¹ they were granted forgiveness for all that they had perpetrated.² But now they began sinning anew. This interpretation is in Agadas Tehilim.

footnotes:
¹ Sung by Devorah and Barak [ch.5].
² Anyone rescued miraculously who then sings a hymn of praise forgiven all his sins, as if he had been newly created. (Yalkut, 60)



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Judges 6:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:2:1)


**הַמִּנְהָרוֹת.** (תרגום:) מַטְמוֹרִיתָא, וּבִלְשׁוֹן לַעַ״ז: וודו״ש, וְעַל שֵׁם שֶׁעוֹשִׂין בָּהֶם מָאוֹר קָטָן בְּמָקוֹם שֶׁאֵין נִיכָּר קוֹרְאָן מִנְהָרוֹת, לויישיט״ש בְּלַעַ״ז:


**Retreats.** Hidden places.³ In old French, "voutes". Because a slight bit of illumination is positioned in them in some inconspicuous spot, they are referred to as מִנְהׇרוֹת, lit. "glimmerings", "lucerno" in old French.

footnotes:
³ This is Targum Yonasan’s translation.


###### Rashi on Judges 6:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:2:2)


**מְעָרוֹת.** גרוטי״ה בְּלַעַ״ז:


**Caves.** "Grotte" in old French


###### Rashi on Judges 6:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:2:3)


**מְצָדוֹת.** פליישדי״ץ בְּלַעַ״ז, שֶׁעוֹשִׂים בַּיְּעָרִים עַל יְדֵי הַפָּלַת הָאִילָנוֹת סָבִיב סָבִיב:


**Stockades.** "Palliser" in old French, "pales", as it is constructed in the forest by felling trees around its perimeter.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ The trees serve as the pales or stakes forming the stockade.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on Judges 6:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:5:1)


**הֵם וּמִקְנֵיהֶם.** לִרְעוֹת אֶת הַתְּבוּאָה:


**With their cattle** to graze on the produce.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on Judges 6:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:8:1)


**אִישׁ נָבִיא.** זֶה הַנָּבִיא הוּא פִּנְחָס, בְּ״סֵדֶר עוֹלָם״ (פרק ב):


**A prophet.** This prophet was Pinchas.⁵ Taken from Seder Olam.

footnotes:
⁵ See above, 2:1.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on Judges 6:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:11:1)


**אֲבִי הָעֶזְרִי.** מִבְּנֵי אֲבִיעֶזֶר בֶּן גִּלְעָד בֶּן מְנַשֶּׁה:


**The Aviezrite.** One of the sons of Aviezer ben Gilod ben Menasheh.


###### Rashi on Judges 6:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:11:2)


**וְגִדְעוֹן בְּנוֹ.** אָבִיו הָיָה חוֹבְטָן וְהוּא כוֹבְרָן, אָמַר לוֹ: אַבָּא, זָקֵן אַתָּה וְאִם יָבֹאוּ הַמִּדְיָנִים לֹא תוּכַל לָנוּס, לֵךְ אַתָּה וַאֲנִי אֶחְבֹּט:


**His son Gidon.** His father had been threshing, while he sifted. He told him, "Father, you are elderly. If the enemies come, you will never be able to escape. You leave, and I will thresh."


###### Rashi on Judges 6:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:11:3)


**בַּגַּת.** בְּקוֹרוֹת בֵּית הַבַּד:


**In the wine press.** With beams of an olive press.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Judges 6:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:12:1)


**ה׳ עִמְּךָ גִּבּוֹר הֶחָיִל.** שֶׁנָּתַן לְךָ כֹּחַ כָּזֶה:


**Adonoy is with you, man of supreme power.** Since He infused you with such power.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Judges 6:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:13:1)


**וְיֵשׁ ה׳ עִמָּנוּ.** וְאִם יֵשׁ ה׳ עִמָּנוּ, לָמָּה מְצָאַתְנוּ וְגוֹ׳:


**If [lit. and] Adonoy is with us.⁶** If Adonoy is with us, why has [all this] occurred, etc.

footnotes:
⁶ Not “And Adonoy is with us,” as the literal reading would indicate, but “If Adonoy is with us,” as demonstrated by the following phrase cited by Rashi, “why has [all this] occurred, etc.”.


###### Rashi on Judges 6:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:13:2)


**אֲשֶׁר סִפְּרוּ לָנוּ אֲבוֹתֵינוּ.** פֶּסַח הָיָה, אָמַר לוֹ: אֶמֶשׁ הִקְרַנִי אַבָּא אֶת הַהַלֵּל, וּשְׁמַעְתִּיו שֶׁהָיָה אוֹמֵר (תהלים קיד:א): בְּצֵאת יִשְׂרָאֵל מִמִּצְרַיִם, ׳וְעַתָּה נְטָשָׁנוּ׳, אִם צַדִּיקִים הָיוּ אֲבוֹתֵינוּ, יַעֲשֶׂה לָנוּ בִּזְכוּתָם, וְאִם רְשָׁעִים הָיוּ, כְּשֵׁם שֶׁעָשָׂה לָהֶם נִפְלְאוֹתָיו חִנָּם כֵּן יַעֲשֶׂה לָנוּ, וְאַיֵּה כָל נִפְלְאוֹתָיו:


**Which our forefathers recounted to us.** It was Pesach, and he told him, "Last night father recited the Hallel for me, and I heard him read, "When Yisroel departed from Egypt"⁷—but now He has abandoned us. If our forefathers were saintly, let Him act in our behalf in their merit; and if they were wicked, then, just as He worked His wonders for them gratuitously, so, let Him act in our behalf. Where are all His marvels?"⁸

footnotes:
⁷ “…the sea observed and fled, the Yardein turned back, etc.” (Tehilim, 114)
⁸ Yalkut, 62.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on Judges 6:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:14:1)


**וַיִּפֶן אֵלָיו.** הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא בְּעַצְמוֹ:


**Turned to him.** The Holy One, blessed is He, Himself.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ Gidon was visited by Adonoy himself in the merit of his advocacy of Yisroel. (Yalkut, ibid)


###### Rashi on Judges 6:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:14:2)


**בְּכֹחֲךָ זֶה.** בְּכֹחַ הַזְּכוּת הַזֶּה שֶׁלִּמַּדְתָּ סָנֵגוֹרְיָא עַל בָּנַי, כָּךְ דָּרַשׁ רַבִּי תַּנְחוּמָא (מדרש תנחומא שופטים ד):


**By this power of yours.** By the power of the merit of your advocacy of my children.¹⁰ This is R' Tanchuma's explanation.

footnotes:
¹⁰ Ibid.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Judges 6:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:15:1)


**הִנֵּה אַלְפִּי.** הָאֶלֶף שֶׁאֲנִי נִמְנֶה בּוֹ לִהְיוֹת תַּחַת שַׂר הָאֶלֶף, הוּא הָאֶלֶף הַדַּל בְּכָל אַלְפֵי מְנַשֶּׁה:


**Behold, my unit [lit. thousand].** The thousand member unit of which I am a constituent, under the command of its captain, is the most inadequate unit among all the units in Menasheh.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on Judges 6:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:17:1)


**שָׁאַתָּה.** כְּמוֹ שֶׁאַתָּה:


**That you.** The same as שֶׁאַתׇּה, with the *segol* vocalization.¹¹ vocalization, which appears in the text, is the same as שֶׁאַתׇּה “that you.”

footnotes:
¹¹ שֶׁאַתׇּה, with the “kamatz”



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on Judges 6:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:19:1)


**קֶמַח מַצּוֹת.** לָמַדְנוּ שֶׁפֶּסַח הָיָה, יוֹם תְּנוּפַת הָעוֹמֶר, לְכָךְ נֶאֱמַר (לקמן ז:יג) וְהִנֵּה צְלִיל לֶחֶם שְׂעוֹרִים מִתְהַפֵּךְ וְגוֹ׳:


**Matzos containing flour.** We learn that it was Pesach, the day the Omer offering was waved.¹² This is why it is said,¹³ "Behold, a toasted barley bread was careening,¹⁴ etc."

footnotes:
¹² On the second day of Pesach. (Vayikra, 23:11)
¹³ Below, 7:13.
¹⁴ “… into the camp of Midyon. It reached the tent and struck it down.” This was in the merit of the Omer offering (Rashi, there, Yalkut, 62), which was made of barley.


###### Rashi on Judges 6:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:19:2)


**וְהַמָּרָק.** וְהָרוֹטֶב:


**Soup.** Broth.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on Judges 6:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:22:1)


**אֲהָהּ.** לְשׁוֹן דְּאָגָה, כְּלוֹמַר מַה תְּהֵא עָלַי:


**Aahha.** This denotes apprehension, as if to say, "What will become of me?"


###### Rashi on Judges 6:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:22:2)


**כִּי עַל כֵּן רָאִיתִי.** כִּי עַל אֲשֶׁר רָאִיתִי מַלְאַךְ ה׳, לְכָךְ אֲנִי דּוֹאֵג וְצוֹעֵק ׳אֲהָהּ׳:


**This, because I have seen.** Because I have seen an angel of Adonoy—this is why I am apprehensive and exclaim "Aahha!"



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on Judges 6:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:24:1)


**וַיִּקְרָא לוֹ.** גִּדְעוֹן לַמִּזְבֵּחַ:


**Entitled it.** Gidon named the altar.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹⁵ The reading is, “[He] entitled it, ‘Adonoy is peace,’” not “Adonoy entitled it, ‘Peace.’”.


###### Rashi on Judges 6:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:24:2)


**ה׳ שָׁלוֹם.** ה׳ הוּא שְׁלוֹמֵנוּ:


**Adonoy is peace.** Adonoy is our peace.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on Judges 6:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:25:1)


**פַּר הַשּׁוֹר.** פַּר הַבָּחוּר, שֶׁהַשּׁוֹר בֶּן יוֹמוֹ קָרוּי שׁוֹר:


**Bullock [lit. bull ox].** The young bull, as even a day old ox is called an "ox".¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁶ The inference of “Bull ox” is a bull that qualifies as an ox. Since even a day old bull qualifies, the reference is to a young bull.


###### Rashi on Judges 6:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:25:2)


**וּפַר הַשֵּׁנִי.** (תרגום:) וְתוֹרָא תִּנְיָנָא:


**A second bull.**וְתוֹרָא תִּנְיׇנׇא, "A second bull".¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁷ This is Targum Yonasan’s rendition.


###### Rashi on Judges 6:25:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:25:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:25:3)


**שֶׁבַע שָׁנִים.** (תרגום:) דְּאִיתְפַּטַּם שֶׁבַע שְׁנִין לַעֲבוֹדָה זָרָה. שְׁמוֹנָה דְבָרִים הוּתְרוּ אוֹתוֹ הַלַּיְלָה: מוּקְצֶה, אֲשֵׁרָה, וְלַיְלָה, וּבָמָה וְכוּ׳, כִּדְאִיתָא בִּתְמוּרָה (כח ב):


**Seven year.** Which had been fattened for seven years to be brought as an idolatrous sacrifice. Eight prohibitions were suspended¹⁸ sacrifices were now used. 3. The wood of the *Asheirah* tree was used as fuel. 4. The offering was brought at night. 5. It was brought on a personal altar outside the Mishkon at Shiloh. 6. The second bull had been actually worshipped as a deity. 7. Gidon was not a Kohein. 8. He did not use the required sacred utensils. that night; 'designated', *'asheirah',* 'night', 'altar', etc., as enumerated in Temurah.¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁸ 1. The second bull, which had been designated as an idolatrous sacrifice, was now brought as an offering. 2. Utensils used previously for the Asheirah
¹⁹ 29:a.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on Judges 6:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:26:1)


**רֹאשׁ הַמָּעוֹז.** רֹאשׁ הַסֶּלַע:


**Top of the bulwark.** Top of the rock.



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on Judges 6:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:31:1)


**הַאַתֶּם תְּרִיבוּן לַבַּעַל.** בִּשְׁבִיל הַבַּעַל:


**You want to fight for [lit. "to"] the baal?** For the sake of the baal.


###### Rashi on Judges 6:31:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:31:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:31:2)


**תּוֹשִׁיעוּן אוֹתוֹ.** תִּנְקְמוּ נִקְמָתוֹ, וְכֵן (שמואל א כה:לג): וְהֹשֵׁעַ יָדִי לִי, דְּדָוִד בְּנָבָל:


**To avenge [lit. rescue] him.** To exact vengeance for him.²⁰ Similarly, "by my own hand exacting my vengeance,"²¹ by David concerning Novol.

footnotes:
²⁰ The literal translation of תּוֹשִׁיעוּן, “to rescue,” does not apply, since the idol was no longer endangered. The alternate interpretation, “to avenge,” is correct here.
²¹ Shmuel 1, 25:33. הוֹשִׁיעָה is interpreted as “avenge.”


###### Rashi on Judges 6:31:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:31:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:31:3)


**אֲשֶׁר יָרִיב לוֹ.** בִּשְׁבִילוֹ:


**Whoever fights for [lit. "to"] him.** For his sake.


###### Rashi on Judges 6:31:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:31:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:31:4)


**עַד הַבֹּקֶר.** עַד הַבֹּקֶר הַמְתִּינוּ:


**Wait until morning.** Until morning, wait.


###### Rashi on Judges 6:31:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:31:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:31:5)


**אִם אֱלֹהִים הוּא.** יָרִיב הוּא לְעַצְמוֹ רִיבוֹ וְנִקְמָתוֹ, וּכְדֵי לִדְחוֹתָם עַד שֶׁיִּבְרַח גִּדְעוֹן הָיָה אוֹמֵר כֵּן:


**If he is a god** let him fight his own battle and exact his own vengeance. To rebuff them until Gidon could escape, he said this.



### Verse 32

###### Rashi on Judges 6:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:32:1)


**יָרֶב בּוֹ.** בְּגִדְעוֹן אֵצֶל רִיב נוֹפֵל עִם, אוֹ אֶת, אוֹ בּוֹ, כְּמוֹ (בראשית לא:לו): וַיָּרֶב בְּלָבָן, אֲבָל ׳לוֹ׳ אֵינוֹ נוֹפֵל עַל הַלָּשׁוֹן, וְכָל ׳לוֹ׳ הַכְּתוּבִים כָּאן ׳בִּשְׁבִיל׳ הֵם:


**Fight against him.** Against Gidon. In relation to רַיב, "fight", the proper term is עִם, or אֶת, or בּוֹ, "with" [him], as in "he fought with Lavan".²² However, לוֹ, lit. "to", is not the proper term. Every לוֹ mentioned here is interpreted as "for his sake."

footnotes:
²² Bereishis, 31:36.



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on Judges 6:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:34:1)


**רוּחַ ה׳.** רוּחַ גְּבוּרָה:


**The Divine spirit.** The spirit of valor.


###### Rashi on Judges 6:34:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:34:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:34:2)


**וַיִּזָּעֵק אֲבִיעֶזֶר.** כָּל הַמִּשְׁפָּחָה:


**Aviezer gathered.** The entire clan.



### Verse 38

###### Rashi on Judges 6:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:38:1)


**וַיְהִי כֵן.** שֶׁהָיָה טַל עַל הַגִּזָּה לְבַדָּהּ יוֹתֵר מִבִּשְׁאָר מְקוֹמוֹת, כְּמוֹ שֶׁהוּא אוֹמֵר (פסוק לז): מְלֹא הַסֵּפֶל מַיִם, אֲבָל ׳עַל כָּל הָאָרֶץ חֹרֶב׳ לֹא נִתְקַיֵּם, שֶׁהַבְּרִית כְּרוּתָה לַטַּל שֶׁאֵינוֹ נֶעְצָר (תענית ג א):


**That is what occurred.** The dew condensed on the fleece alone more than everywhere else, as it is said, "a full bowl of water." However, "while all of the ground is dry" did not materialize, as a Divine covenant assures perpetual dew fall.


###### Rashi on Judges 6:38:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:38:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/6:38:2)


**וַיָּזַר.** לְשׁוֹן מְכַבֵּשׁ, וְכֵן (ישעיהו א:ו): לֹא זֹרוּ וְלֹא חֻבָּשׁוּ, לֹא נֶחְבְּשׁוּ לְהוֹצִיא הַלֵּחָה:


**He squeezed.** The word connotes pressure. Similarly, "They were neither pressed nor bandaged."²³ They were not strapped in bandages to draw out the discharge.

footnotes:
²³ Yeshaya, 1:6.



## Chapter 7


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Judges 7:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:1:1)


**מִגִּבְעַת הַמּוֹרֶה בָּעֵמֶק.** (תרגום:) דְּמִסְתַּכְּיָא לְמֵישְׁרָא. הַמּוֹרֶה, לְשׁוֹן הוֹרָאָה וּרְאִיָּה, כְּמוֹ (משלי ו:יג) מוֹרֶה בְּאֶצְבְּעוֹתָיו, שֶׁמִּשָּׁם צוֹפִים וְרוֹמְזִים בָּעֵמֶק:


**Of signal valley ridge.** Overlooking the plain¹. הַמּוֹרֶה connotes instruction, observation, as in "instructs by gesturing."² From there they would observe, and then signal instructions to the valley.

footnotes:
¹ This is Targum Yonasan’s interpretation. The ridge overlooked the plain, and was thus suitable as an observation post.
² Mishlei, 6:13.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Judges 7:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:2:1)


**יִתְפָּאֵר.** וונטי״ר בְּלַעַ״ז:


**Glorify.** "Vanter" in old French.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on Judges 7:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:3:1)


**וַיִּצְפֹּר.** בַּבֹּקֶר, לְשׁוֹן אֲרָמִי: צַפְרָא:


**In the morning.** In the morning is צַפְדׇא in Aramaic.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on Judges 7:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:5:1)


**כֹּל אֲשֶׁר יָלֹק בִּלְשׁוֹנוֹ וְגוֹ׳ וְכֹל אֲשֶׁר יִכְרַע עַל בִּרְכָּיו לִשְׁתּוֹת.** תַּצִּיג אוֹתוֹ לְבַד חוּץ מִסִּיעָתְךָ, כִּי הֵם לֹא יֵלְכוּ עִמְּךָ, שֶׁכָּךְ הֵם לְמוּדִים לִכְרוֹעַ לִפְנֵי עֲבוֹדַת גִּלּוּלִים:


**Anyone who laps with his tongue, etc.** Anyone who bends forward while on his knees to drink, set them aside, out of your retinue. They shall not accompany you, since they are practiced in kneeling before idols.³

footnotes:
³ Both phrases of this passage—“Anyone who laps, etc.” and “anyone who bends forward, etc.”—refer to the same drinkers. Those who bend forward while on their knees, bringing their faces down to the water in order to lap it up, are practiced in prostrating themselves before idols.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on Judges 7:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:6:1)


**הַמְלַקְקִים בְּיָדָם.** אֵין זֶה דֶּרֶךְ כְּרִיעָה כְּלוֹקֵק בִּלְשׁוֹנוֹ:


**Who lapped from hand.** This is not the kind of bending forward as when lapping up with the tongue.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ Lapping from hand to mouth does not involve prostrating oneself.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Judges 7:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:12:1)


**נֹפְלִים בָּעֵמֶק.** שׁוֹכְנִים בָּעֵמֶק:


**Lay in the valley.** Were stationed in the valley.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Judges 7:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:13:1)


**צְלִיל.** צָלוּל כְּתִיב, שֶׁהָיָה הַדּוֹר צָלוּל מִן הַצַּדִּיקִים:


**Toasted.** The written text is צׇלוּל, "clear".⁵ The generation was cleared of righteous men.

footnotes:
⁵ Occasionally, the written Scriptural text, כְתַב, differs from the the spoken text, קֽרִי. Rashi notes that this is the case here, where the written text is צׇלוּל, while the spoken text is צְלִיל, “toasted”.


###### Rashi on Judges 7:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:13:2)


**צְלִיל לֶחֶם שְׂעֹרִים.** (תרגום:) חֲרַר דְּלֶחֶם שְׂעוֹרִים, חֲרָרָה שֶׁאוֹפִין עַל הַגֶּחָלִים:


**A toasted barley bread.** A loaf of barley bread. A loaf toasted over coals.


###### Rashi on Judges 7:13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:13:3)


**לֶחֶם שְׂעֹרִים.** הוּא זְכוּת הָעוֹמֶר הַקָּרֵב בְּפֶסַח:


**Barley bread.** This represents the merit of the Omer offering, which was brought on Pesach.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ On the second day of Pesach. The offering consisted of barley meal. (Vayikra, 23:11,16. See Rashi there. Cf. Rashi above, 6:19).


###### Rashi on Judges 7:13:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:13:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:13:4)


**וַיַּכֵּהוּ וַיִּפֹּל.** הָאֹהֶל: **וַיַּהַפְכֵהוּ.** הָאֹהֶל מִלְּמַטָּה לְמַעְלָה, וְנָפַל הָאֹהֶל:


**And hit it, and it collapsed** (the tent), **then it overturned** the tent, completely overturned, and the tent collapsed.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Judges 7:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:15:1)


**אֶת מִסְפַּר הַחֲלוֹם.** אֶת סִפּוּר הַחֲלוֹם, (תרגום:) יַת שׁוּעִית חֶלְמָא וְיַת פִּשְׁרֵיהּ:


**The recounting of the dream.** The story of the dream.⁷ "The retelling of the dream and its interpretation."⁸

footnotes:
⁷ מִסְפַר, generally translated as “number”, is interpreted here as “story”.
⁸ This is Targum Yonasan’s translation.


###### Rashi on Judges 7:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:15:2)


**וְאֶת שִׁבְרוֹ.** וְאֶת מִמְכָּרוֹ, כְּלוֹמַר: וְאֶת פִּתְרוֹנוֹ:


**And its interpretation [lit. "its sale"].** The goods exchanged for it,⁹ or its interpretation.

footnotes:
⁹ According to the literal interpretation, “sale”.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on Judges 7:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:16:1)


**שׁוֹפָרוֹת וְלַפִּידִים.** לְהַזְכִּיר זְכוּת מַתַּן תּוֹרָה וּפְשׁוּטוֹ: לַיְלָה הָיָה, כְּמוֹ שֶׁכָּתוּב (פסוק ט) וְהָיָה חֹשֶׁךְ, לְפִיכָךְ נָשְׂאוּ שָׁם לַפִּידִים לְהָאִיר לָהֶם וּנְתָנוּם בַּכַּדִּים כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יָבִינוּ בַּלַּפִּידִים:


**Shofars and torches** to invoke the merit of the giving of the Torah.¹⁰ According to the plain explanation: this took place at night, as it is written,¹¹ and it was dark. They therefore carried torches to provide them with light. They placed them in pitchers so that the torches would remain undetected.

footnotes:
¹⁰ At Mount Sinai, when the shofar sounded, and fire descended on the mountain. (Shemos, 19:16–19).
¹¹ V. 9.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on Judges 7:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:17:1)


**מִמֶּנִּי תִרְאוּ.** אֶת אֲשֶׁר אֲנִי עוֹשֶׂה, רְאוּ וַעֲשׂוּ כֵן:


**Watch me.** Watch my actions, and follow suit.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on Judges 7:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:18:1)


**אָנֹכִי וְכָל אֲשֶׁר אִתִּי.** אֶחָד מִן הָרָאשִׁים, וּמֵאָה אִישׁ הָיָה עִמּוֹ (פסוק יט):


**I, and everyone with me.** One of the divisions and one hundred men accompanied him.


###### Rashi on Judges 7:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:18:2)


**לַה׳ וּלְגִדְעוֹן.** (תרגום:) חַרְבָּא דִּמְקַטְּלָא מִן קֳדָם ה׳ וְנִצְחָנָא עַל יְדֵי גִדְעוֹן:


**For Adonoy, and for Gidon.** The sword that executes is from Adonoy, so that victory is Gidon's.¹²

footnotes:
¹² This is Targum Yonasan’s interpretation, following v. 20, “Adonoy’s sword, and for Gidon!”



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on Judges 7:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:19:1)


**הָקֵם הֵקִימוּ.** כְּבָר בְּנֵי הַחַיִל אֶת הַשּׁוֹמְרִים שֶׁהָיְתָה מִשְׁמַרְתָּם בָּאַשְׁמֹרֶת הַתִּיכוֹנָה, שֶׁכֵּן דֶּרֶךְ בְּנֵי גְיָיסוֹת לְהַעֲמִיד שׁוֹמְרִים, אֵלּוּ יִשְׁמְרוּ שְׁלִישׁ הַלַּיְלָה הָרִאשׁוֹן וְאֵלּוּ שְׁלִישׁ הַשֵּׁנִי, וְאֵלּוּ שְׁלִישׁ הָאַחֲרוֹן:


**Had posted.** The soldiers had already posted the sentries who were assigned to the middle watch. Members of the military usually assign sentry duty so that some serve during the first third of the night, some during the second third, and some during the final third.¹³

footnotes:
¹³ See Yalkut, 63.


###### Rashi on Judges 7:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:19:2)


**וְנָפוֹץ הַכַּדִּים.** וְלִשְׁבּוֹר הַכַּדִּים אֲשֶׁר בְּיָדָם:


**And smashed the pitchers.** And broke¹⁴ the pitchers which were in their hands.

footnotes:
¹⁴ נׇפוֹץ is sometimes translated as “dispersed”. Here, however, the translation is שָבוֹר, “broke”, as stated clearly in v.20.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on Judges 7:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:21:1)


**וַיָּרִיעוּ.** תְּרוּעַת מַסָּע וְנִיסָה:


**They sounded the alarm.** The signal to withdraw and flee.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on Judges 7:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:24:1)


**וְלִכְדוּ לָהֶם אֶת הַמַּיִם.** הַמַּפְסִיקִין בֵּין אֲרָם לְאֶרֶץ כְּנָעַן:


**Capture the waters** which intervene between Aram and Canaanite territory.


###### Rashi on Judges 7:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:24:2)


**וְאֶת הַיַּרְדֵּן.** שֶׁגַּם הוּא הָיָה מַפְסִיק:


**And the Yardein** which also intervened.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on Judges 7:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/7:25:1)


**הֵבִיאוּ אֶל גִּדְעוֹן מֵעֵבֶר לַיַּרְדֵּן.** בַּבֹּקֶר, כְּשֶׁעָבַר גִּדְעוֹן אֶת הַיַּרְדֵּן לִרְדֹּף אַחַר זֶבַח וְצַלְמֻנָּע:


**They brought to Gidon, across the Yardein.** In the morning, when Gidon crossed the Yardein pursuing Zevach and Tzalmona.¹⁵



## Chapter 8


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Judges 8:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:2:1)


**מַה עָשִׂיתִי עַתָּה כָּכֶם.** מַה חָשׁוּב מַה שֶּׁעָשִׂיתִי בִּתְחִלָּתוֹ, כְּמַה שֶּׁעֲשִׂיתֶם אַתֶּם בַּסּוֹף:


**Can any action of mine compare with yours?** How significant were my actions at the beginning in comparison with yours at the end?


###### Rashi on Judges 8:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:2:2)


**הֲלֹא טוֹב עֹלְלוֹת.** שֶׁבָּאוּ לְיֶדְכֶם בַּסּוֹף מִתְּחִלַּת הַבָּצִיר שֶׁבָּצַרְנוּ אֲנִי וּמִשְׁפַּחְתִּי, שֶׁהֲרֵי בְּיֶדְכֶם נָתַן ה׳ אֶת הַמְּלָכִים:


**Do not Ephraim's underripened gleanings surpass.** The gleanings¹ which you acquired at the end surpass the early harvest which my family and I gathered, for Adonoy delivered the kings into your hands.

footnotes:
¹ עוֹלְלוֹת are underripened grapes which may not be harvested, but must be left to be gleaned afterward by the poor. (Vayikra, 19:10) Gidon compared his original battle with the Midyonites to the harvest, and the ensuing execution of Orev and Z’ev to the gleaning of the עוֹלְלוֹת.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on Judges 8:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:3:1)


**וּמַה יָּכֹלְתִּי.** לַעֲשׂוֹת שֶׁיְּהֵא חָשׁוּב כְּמַעֲשֵׂה שֶׁלָּכֶם:


**What could I have** done that would have been as significant as your accomplishments?



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Judges 8:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:4:1)


**עֹבֵר הוּא וּשְׁלֹשׁ מֵאוֹת הָאִישׁ.** עוֹבְרִים אֶת הַיַּרְדֵּן אַחֲרֵי זֶבַח וְצַלְמֻנָּע שֶׁעָבְרוּ אֶת הַיַּרְדֵּן תְּחִלָּה, וְהָיוּ מַשְׁחִיתִים מֵעֵבֶר לַיַּרְדֵּן אֶת אֶרֶץ רְאוּבֵן וְגָד וּמְנַשֶּׁה, וְעָבַר שָׁם כְּדֵי לִנְקֹם אַף בָּהֶם:


**He crossed with the three hundred men.** They crossed the Yardein after Zevach and Tzalmuna, who had crossed previously and wreaked destruction across the Yardein, in the territory of Reuven, Gad, and Menashe. He crossed to take revenge against them as well.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on Judges 8:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:5:1)


**לְאַנְשֵׁי סֻכּוֹת.** יִשְׂרָאֵל הָיוּ:


**To the people of Succos.** They were Yisroelites.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on Judges 8:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:6:1)


**הֲכַף זֶבַח וְצַלְמֻנָּע בְּיָדֶךָ.** שֶׁאַתָּה מִתְפָּאֵר שֶׁהִצַּלְתָּנוּ מִיַּד מִדְיָן:


**Are the palms of Zevach and Tzalmuna** in your hands that you pride yourself in having rescued us from the hand of Midyon.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on Judges 8:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:7:1)


**הַבַּרְקָנִים.** רונצי״ש בְּלַעַ״ז:


**Thorns.** "Ronzes" in old French.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on Judges 8:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:10:1)


**בְּקַרְקֹר.** שֵׁם מָקוֹם:


**At Karkor.** The name of a place.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on Judges 8:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:11:1)


**דֶּרֶךְ הַשֹּׁכְנִי בָאֳהָלִים.** דֶּרֶךְ אֶרֶץ קֵדָר וַעֲרָב הַשּׁוֹכְנִים בַּמִּדְבָּר בְּאָהֳלֵיהֶם, שֶׁרוֹעִים מִקְנֶה תָּמִיד וְאֵין דָּרִים בְּבָתִּים:


**By way of the tent dwellers.** By way of the lands of Kedar and Arabia, whose inhabitants were wilderness tent dwellers. Since they were occupied exclusively as shepherds, they could not dwell in houses.


###### Rashi on Judges 8:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:11:2)


**מִקֶּדֶם לְנֹבַח וְיָגְבֳּהָה.** שֶׁהֵם עָרֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל, וְלֹא רָצָה לֵילֵךְ שָׁם אֶלָּא דֶּרֶךְ עֲקַלָּתוֹן, כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יַרְגִּישׁוּ בָהֶם, וִיהוּמַּם פִּתְאוֹם:


**East of Novach and Yagbeha.** These were Yisroelite cities. He chose not to pass through them, but rather travelled roundabout, so that they would remain undetected, and could overwhelm them by surprise.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Judges 8:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:13:1)


**מִלְמַעְלָה הֶחָרֶס.** עַד שֶׁלֹּא שָׁקְעָה הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ, בְּעוֹד הַחַמָּה נִרְאֵית גָּבוֹהַּ:


**With the sun high.** Before sunset, with the sun still visible high above the horizon.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on Judges 8:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:16:1)


**וַיֹּדַע בָּהֶם.** (תרגום:) וּתְבַר בְּהוֹן:


**Tore with them.** "Broke apart" with them.²

footnotes:
² This is Targum Yonasan’s translation.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on Judges 8:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:18:1)


**אֶחָד כְּתֹאַר בְּנֵי הַמֶּלֶךְ.** יוֹנָתָן תִּרְגֵּם: אֶחָד הָיָה בָהֶם שֶׁתּוֹאֲרוֹ כְּתוֹאַר בְּנֵי הַמֶּלֶךְ. וְיֵשׁ לְפוֹתְרוֹ: כָּמוֹךָ כָּמוֹהֶם, תּוֹאַר אֶחָד לָכֶם, כִּי כְתוֹאַר בְּנֵי מְלָכִים הָיוּ:


**Singular, of princely appearance.** Yonasan explains that one of them was of princely appearance.³ However, this may be interpreted as, "They were exactly like you, all of singular appearance, for they were of princely appearance."

footnotes:
³ Yonasan’s reading is: “One was exactly like you, of princely appearance.”



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on Judges 8:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:20:1)


**וְלֹא שָׁלַף הַנַּעַר חַרְבּוֹ.** לְהָרְגָם, שֶׁהָיָה יָרֵא מֵהֶם:


**But the boy would not draw his sword** to execute them, for he was afraid of them.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on Judges 8:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:21:1)


**הַשַּׂהֲרֹנִים.** לוינ״ש בְּלַעַ״ז, שֶׁל זָהָב:


**Crescent chains.** Lunettes in old French, moonshaped, made of gold.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on Judges 8:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:26:1)


**הַשַּׂהֲרֹנִים וְהַנְּטִיפוֹת.** (תרגום:) עִנְקְיָא וּכְלִילָא:


**The crescent chains and crowns.** The neckbands and crowns.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ This is Targum Yonasan’s rendition.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on Judges 8:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:27:1)


**וַיַּעַשׂ אוֹתוֹ גִדְעוֹן לְאֵפוֹד.** לִהְיוֹת לְזִכָּרוֹן עַל תְּשׁוּעָה גְדוֹלָה, לִרְאוֹת כַּמָּה הָיָה חֵילָם כָּבֵד שֶׁבְּנִזְמֵי חֲשׁוּבֵיהֶם הָיָה כָּל הַזָּהָב הַזֶּה:


**Gidon fashioned it into an apron** as a memorial to the the great salvation, demonstrating how enormous their forces were, as the nose rings of their eminent men contained so much gold.


###### Rashi on Judges 8:27:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:27:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:27:2)


**וַיִּזְנוּ כָל יִשְׂרָאֵל אַחֲרָיו.** לְאַחַר מִיתַת גִּדְעוֹן:


**All of Yisroel were perverted towards it** after Gidon's demise.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ But not during his lifetime. Gidon’s intentions were pure. (See Yalkut, 64.)



### Verse 33

###### Rashi on Judges 8:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:33:1)


**בַּעַל בְּרִית.** כָּךְ שְׁמוֹ:


**Baal beris.** This was his name.



### Verse 35

###### Rashi on Judges 8:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/8:35:1)


**וְלֹא עָשׂוּ חֶסֶד וְגוֹ׳.** שֶׁהָרְגוּ אֶת בָּנָיו, כְּמוֹ שֶׁכָּתוּב בָּעִנְיָן (שופטים ט:ה):


**They did not act benevolently.** They murdered his children, as stated in the narrative.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Ch. 9.



## Chapter 9


### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Judges 9:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:4:1)


**וּפֹחֲזִים.** בְּהוּלִים בְּמַעֲשֵׂיהֶם לַעֲשׂוֹת בְּלֹא תְבוּנָה:


**Headstrong.** They acted impetuously, thoughtlessly.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on Judges 9:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:6:1)


**עִם אֵלוֹן מֻצָּב.** (תרגום:) מֵישַׁר קָמְתָא - מִישׁוֹר שֶׁהָיוּ בּוֹ מַצֵּבוֹת:


**Near the plain of the pillar.** "The plain of monuments"—¹ A plain in which monuments stood.

footnotes:
¹ This is Targum Yonasan’s translation.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on Judges 9:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:8:1)


**הָלוֹךְ הָלְכוּ הָעֵצִים.** מָשָׁל הוּא:


**The trees went about resolutely.** This is a parable.²

footnotes:
² Yalkut, 65.


###### Rashi on Judges 9:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:8:2)


**לַזַּיִת.** עָתְנִיאֵל בֶּן קְנַז (שופטים א:יג), הַבָּא מִשֵּׁבֶט יְהוּדָה שֶׁנִּקְרָא זַיִת, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (ירמיהו יא:טז): זַיִת רַעֲנָן יְפֵה פְרִי תֹאַר וְגוֹ׳:


**The olive tree.** Asniel ben Kenaz,³ a tribesman of Judah, who are entitled "olive", as it is said, "Refreshed olive tree, with fruit beautiful in appearance, etc."⁴

footnotes:
³ Above, 3; 9–11. (Yalkut, ibid)
⁴ Yirmiyahu, 11:16. The chapter is addressed to “the men of Yehudah” (v.1).



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on Judges 9:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:9:1)


**דִּשְׁנִי.** לְשׁוֹן שׁוּמָן:


**The richness of my oil.** The connotation here is fattiness.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on Judges 9:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:10:1)


**לַתְּאֵנָה.** דְּבוֹרָה:


**To the fig tree.** This refers to Devorah.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ Above, 4:4. (Yalkut, ibid.)



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on Judges 9:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:11:1)


**אֶת מָתְקִי.** דְּבַשׁ דְּבוֹרִים:


**My sweetness.** the honey of dates.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Judges 9:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:12:1)


**לַגֶּפֶן.** גִּדְעוֹן, שֶׁהוּא מִזֶּרַע יוֹסֵף (שופטים ו:יא), שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בּוֹ (בראשית מט:כב) בֵּן פֹּרָת, (ותרגם שם אונקלוס:) כְּגוּפָן דִּנְצִיב וְכוּ׳:


**To the grapevine.** This refers to Gidon,⁶ a descendant of Yoseif, of whom it is said, "A flourishing son"—"like a grapevine positioned, etc."⁷

footnotes:
⁶ Yalkut, ibid.
⁷ “A flourishing son over a wellspring”. (Bereishis, 49:22). Targum Onkelos there interprets, “like a grapevine positioned over a wellspring.”



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Judges 9:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:13:1)


**הַמְשַׂמֵּחַ אֱלֹהִים.** שֶׁאֵין אוֹמְרִים הַלְוִיִּם שִׁיר שֶׁל הַקָּרְבָּן אֶלָּא עַל הַנְּסָכִים:


**Which brings joy to God.** As the Levi'im chant the hymns of glory only during the wine libation ceremony.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Berachos, 35:b.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on Judges 9:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:14:1)


**אֶל הָאָטָד.** ספינ״ה בְּלַעַ״ז:


**The thornbush.** "epine" in old French.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Judges 9:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:15:1)


**אִם בֶּאֱמֶת.** שֶׁאֲנִי הָגוּן לַמַּלְכוּת:


**If you genuinely.** Because I qualify as a sovereign.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on Judges 9:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:22:1)


**וַיָּשַׂר.** עַל כָּרְחָם נָהַג רַבָּנוּת וּבְגַאֲוָה:


**Ruled.** He wielded his authority through repression, and conducted himself arrogantly.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on Judges 9:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:23:1)


**רוּחַ רָעָה.** שִׂנְאָה, שלנ״ש מלטלינ״ט בְּלַעַ״ז:


**A malicious sentiment.** Hatred. "Talent" in old French.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on Judges 9:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:26:1)


**גַּעַל בֶּן עֶבֶד.** מֵאוּמָּה אַחֶרֶת הָיָה:


**Ga'al ben Eved.** He was from another nation.



### Verse 27

###### Rashi on Judges 9:27:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:27:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:27:1)


**וַיַּעֲשׂוּ הִלּוּלִים.** (תרגום:) וַעֲבַדוּ חַנְגִין:


**Indulged in carousals.** Performed dances.⁹

footnotes:
⁹ This is Targum Yonasan’s translation.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on Judges 9:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:28:1)


**מִי אֲבִימֶלֶךְ וּמִי שְׁכֶם.** מִי אֲבִימֶלֶךְ לִהְיוֹת שַׂר עַל שְׁכֶם וּמִי שְׁכֶם לִהְיוֹת עֲבָדִים לַאֲבִימֶלֶךְ, הֲלֹא בֶּן יְרֻבַּעַל הָיָה אֲבִימֶלֶךְ מִן עָפְרַת אֲבִי הָעֶזְרִי (לעיל ו:יא):


**Who is Avimelech, and who is Shechem.** Who is Avimelech to control Shechem, and who is Shechem to be subjugated to Avimelech? Is not Avimelech Yerubaal's son, from Ofros, of the Aviezrites?¹⁰


###### Rashi on Judges 9:28:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:28:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:28:2)


**וּזְבֻל פְּקִידוֹ.** וְזֶה פְּקִיד הָעִיר שֶׁשְּׁמוֹ ׳זְבוּל׳, פְּקִידוֹ שֶׁל אֲבִימֶלֶךְ הוּא, וַאֲדוֹנָיו אֵין לוֹ מִשְׁפַּט מְלוּכָה בָּעִיר וְאַף פְּקִידוֹ אֵינוֹ כְּלוּם:


**And his henchman Zevul?** The city superintendent, named Zevul, is Avimelech's henchman. His master cannot justify his authority over the city; thus his appointee, as well, is a non-entity.


###### Rashi on Judges 9:28:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:28:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:28:3)


**עִבְדוּ אֶת אַנְשֵׁי חֲמוֹר.** אִם בָּאתֶם לִקְנוֹת לָכֶם אֲדוֹנִים, בֹּאוּ וְעִבְדוּ אֶת אַנְשֵׁי חֲמוֹר, שֶׁהָיָה נְשִׂיא הָאָרֶץ מִקֶּדֶם (בראשית לד:ב):


**Serve the people of Chamor.** If you want to get yourselves leaders, come serve the men of Chamor, who ruled the land in early times.¹¹

footnotes:
¹¹ Bereishis, 34:2.


###### Rashi on Judges 9:28:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:28:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:28:4)


**וּמַדּוּעַ נַעַבְדֶנּוּ.** לַאֲבִימֶלֶךְ:


**But why must we serve him?** Avimelech.



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on Judges 9:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:29:1)


**וַיֹּאמֶר לַאֲבִימֶלֶךְ.** וַיֹּאמֶר גַּעַל בִּפְנֵי זְבוּל, הוֹדִיעַ לַאֲבִימֶלֶךְ:


**He said: For Avimelech.** Ga'al declared in the presence of Zevul, "Tell Avimelech."


###### Rashi on Judges 9:29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:29:2)


**רַבֶּה צְבָאֲךָ וָצֵאָה.** מִן הַמָּקוֹם שֶׁאַתָּה שָׁם, וְנִרְאֶה אֶת גְּדֻלָּתְךָ:


**Beef up your army and come on out** from your place, and let us see how grand you are!



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on Judges 9:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:31:1)


**בְּתָרְמָה.** כְּמוֹ בְּמִרְמָה, בְּתַרְמִית, כְּלוֹמַר: בַּלָּט וּבַסֵּתֶר:


**Clandestinely.** As in "cunningly,"¹² "in deceit,"¹³ meaning secretively, stealthily.

footnotes:
¹² Bereishis, 27:35.
¹³ Yirmiyahu, 8:5.



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on Judges 9:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:34:1)


**אַרְבָּעָה רָאשִׁים.** אַרְבָּעָה כִּתּוֹת:


**Four divisions.** Four companies.



### Verse 37

###### Rashi on Judges 9:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:37:1)


**מֵעִם טַבּוּר הָאָרֶץ.** (תרגום:) מִן תּוּקְפָא דְאַרְעָא, מֵהַר הַגָּבוֹהַּ שֶׁבְּכֻלָּם:


**From the promontory of the land.** "From the pre-eminence of the land."¹⁴ From the highest summit of all.

footnotes:
¹⁴ This is Targum Yonasan’s translation.


###### Rashi on Judges 9:37:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:37:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:37:2)


**אֵלוֹן מְעוֹנְנִים** (תרגום:) מֵישַׁר מְעוֹנְנִים:


**Elon Meonenim.** The plain of Meonenim



### Verse 42

###### Rashi on Judges 9:42:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:42:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:42:1)


**וַיֵּצֵא הָעָם הַשָּׂדֶה.** אַנְשֵׁי שְׁכֶם:


**The people went out to the fields.** The people of Shechem.


###### Rashi on Judges 9:42:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:42:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:42:2)


**וַיַּגִּידוּ.** הַמַּגִּידִים לַאֲבִימֶלֶךְ, שֶׁיָּצְאוּ בְּנֵי הָעִיר אֶל הַשָּׂדֶה לַעֲשׂוֹת חֶפְצֵיהֶם:


**They informed.** Those who gave the information to Avimelech, that the townspeople had gone to the fields to attend to their wants.



### Verse 44

###### Rashi on Judges 9:44:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:44:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:44:1)


**פֶּתַח שַׁעַר הָעִיר.** שֶׁלֹּא יוּכְלוּ לִיכָּנֵס:


**At the entrance to the gateway of the city** to block their entry.



### Verse 46

###### Rashi on Judges 9:46:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:46:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:46:1)


**צְרִיחַ.** יַעַר שֶׁכָּפוּ אִילָנוֹת וּפְסָגִים סָבִיב סָבִיב, וְקוֹרִין אוֹתוֹ פלייש״ר בְּלַעַ״ז:


**Forest stockade.** A forest encircled by twisted trees and branches, called "plisse" in old French.


###### Rashi on Judges 9:46:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:46:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:46:2)


**בֵּית אֵל בְּרִית.** אוֹתוֹ צְרִיחַ הָיָה שֶׁל בַּעַל בְּרִית, שֵׁם שֶׁל עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה שֶׁלָּהֶם, וְצַלְמוֹן (פסוק מח), יַעַר הָיָה שָׁם וְיֵשׁ פּוֹתְרִין: צְרִיחַ, וודו״י בְּלַעַ״ז שֶׁעוֹשִׂין בַּקַּרְקַע:


**Of the temple of the god Beris.** The stockade belonged to Baal Beris, the name of their deity. Tzalmon¹⁵, too, owned a forest there. Others interpret **צֽרִיחַ** as "voutes" in old French, "bunker," dug into the ground.

footnotes:
¹⁵ Mentioned in v. 48.



### Verse 48

###### Rashi on Judges 9:48:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:48:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:48:1)


**שׂוֹכַת עֵצִים.** עֲנַף עֵצִים:


**The overhang of a tree.** The branch of a tree.



### Verse 53

###### Rashi on Judges 9:53:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:53:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:53:1)


**פֶּלַח רֶכֶב.** חֲתִיכַת אֶבֶן שֶׁל רֵחַיִם הָעֶלְיוֹנָה הַקְּרוּיָה רֶכֶב:


**A pestle shard.** A fragment of upper grinding stone called "pestle" [lit. "rider.¹⁶].

footnotes:
¹⁶ As the upper pestle stone “rides” the lower mortar stone. (Metzudos).


###### Rashi on Judges 9:53:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:53:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:53:2)


**וַתָּרִץ.** וַתְּרוֹצֵץ:


**And smashed.** Crushed.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁷ תָּרָץ is usually translated as “ran”. Here, however, the translation is “crushed”, or “smashed”.



### Verse 54

###### Rashi on Judges 9:54:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:54:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/9:54:1)


**פֶּן יֹאמְרוּ לִי.** פֶּן יֹאמְרוּ עָלַי:


**Lest it be said of me.** Lest it be said concerning me.¹⁸

footnotes:
¹⁸ לי is not translated as “to me,” but as “concerning me.”



## Chapter 10


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Judges 10:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:1:1)


**בֶּן דּוֹדוֹ.** כָּךְ שְׁמוֹ:


**Ben Dodo.** This was his name.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Judges 10:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:4:1)


**עֲיָרִים.** (תרגום:) עוּלִין, סוּסִים בְּחוּרִים:


**Thoroughbreds.****עוּלִין**,¹ the choicest horses.

footnotes:
¹ This is Targum Yonasan’s translation.


###### Rashi on Judges 10:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:4:2)


**וּשְׁלֹשִׁים עֲיָרִים לָהֶם.** וּשְׁלֹשִׁים עָרִים בְּלֹא חוֹמָה לָהֶם, לְכָךְ נִקְרְאוּ עֲיָרִים, לְשׁוֹן עֲיָירוֹת:


**They owned thirty towns.** They owned thirty unwalled municipalities. This is why they are described as **עַיָרִים**, "towns", meaning the same as **עַיָירָוֹת**.²

footnotes:
² Rather than עָרִים meaning walled cities.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on Judges 10:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:6:1)


**אֶת הַבְּעָלִים וְגוֹ׳.** שֶׁבַע עֲבוֹדוֹת זָרוֹת נִמְנוּ כָּאן:


**The Baals.** Seven pagan gods are enumerated here.³

footnotes:
³ Beitzah, 25:b. “The deities of Aram, Tzidon, etc.” does not explain where the Baals and Ashteros were worshiped, but lists deities in addition to them. Hence, the total is seven.


###### Rashi on Judges 10:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:6:2)


**וַיַּעַזְבוּ אֶת ה׳ וְלֹא עֲבָדוּהוּ.** אֲפִילוּ בְּשׁוּתָּפוּת עִם אֵלּוּ:


**They abandoned Adonoy,** and did not worship Him. Not even concurrently with these.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ Otherwise, “and did not worship Him” is redundant. (Beitzah, ibid).



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on Judges 10:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:8:1)


**בַּשָּׁנָה הַהִיא.** כְּשֶׁמֵּת יָאִיר:


**That year** when Yair died.


###### Rashi on Judges 10:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:8:2)


**אֲשֶׁר בְּעֵבֶר הַיַּרְדֵּן.** אֶרֶץ רְאוּבֵן וְגָד וַחֲצִי הַמְּנַשֶּׁה, שֶׁהָיוּ שְׁכֵנֵיהֶם:


**Across the Yardein.** The territory of Reuven, Gad, and half of Menasheh; they were neighbors.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ Of the Ammonites. See below, ch.11.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on Judges 10:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:11:1)


**הֲלֹא מִמִּצְרַיִם.** שֶׁבַע תְּשׁוּעוֹת יֵשׁ כָּאן, כְּנֶגֶד שֶׁבַע עֲבוֹדוֹת זָרוֹת שֶׁהָיוּ עוֹבְדִים:


**Was it not from Mitzrayim.** There are seven salvations here, corresponding with the seven pagan gods they worshiped.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ V. 6. See Rashi there.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Judges 10:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:12:1)


**וּמָעוֹן.** שֵׁם אוּמָּה:


**And Maon.** The name of a nation.⁷

footnotes:
⁷ But not, as generally translated, “a dwelling place.”



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on Judges 10:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:16:1)


**וַתִּקְצַר נַפְשׁוֹ בַּעֲמַל.** כִּבְיָכוֹל לֹא הָיָה רַחֲבַת מָקוֹם לִסְבּוֹל וְלִכָּנֵס שָׁם צָרָה שֶׁהָיָה לוֹ בַּעֲמַל יִשְׂרָאֵל:


**Could not contain Himself at [Yisroel's] anguish.** As if one could depict God as lacking the space to bear and encompass His pain at Yisroel's anguish.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on Judges 10:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/10:17:1)


**וַיִּצָּעֲקוּ.** נֶאֶסְפוּ עַל יְדֵי כָּרוּז:


**Were called together.** They were assembled by proclamation.



## Chapter 11


### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Judges 11:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:4:1)


**וַיִּלָּחֲמוּ בְנֵי עַמּוֹן.** הִיא הָאֲמוּרָה לְמַעְלָה (י:ט):


**The Bnei Ammon waged war.** As mentioned above.¹



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on Judges 11:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:11:1)


**אֶת כָּל דְּבָרָיו.** הַתְּנַאי שֶׁבֵּינֵיהֶם:


**All his stipulations.** The conditions between them.


###### Rashi on Judges 11:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:11:2)


**לִפְנֵי ה׳ בַּמִּצְפָּה.** לְמָקוֹם שֶׁנֶּאֶסְפוּ כֻּלָּם, שֶׁהַשְּׁכִינָה שׁוֹרָה עַל רוֹב צִבּוּר:


**Before Adonoy, at Mitzpah.** Where everyone was assembled, as the Divine Presence rests upon multitudes.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on Judges 11:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:16:1)


**עַד יַם סוּף.** בִּדְרוֹמָהּ שֶׁל אֶרֶץ אֱדוֹם, וְאֶרֶץ אֱדוֹם בִּדְרוֹמָהּ שֶׁל אֶרֶץ כְּנַעַן:


**Until the Reed Sea.** South of the land of Edom, while the land of Edom is south of the land of Canaan.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on Judges 11:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:17:1)


**אֶעְבְּרָה נָּא בְאַרְצֶךָ.** מִדָּרוֹם לְצָפוֹן, לִיכָּנֵס לְאֶרֶץ כְּנַעַן:


**Please allow me to traverse your country.** From south to north, in order to enter the land of Canaan.


###### Rashi on Judges 11:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:17:2)


**וְגַם אֶל מֶלֶךְ מוֹאָב.** שֶׁהָיְתָה אַרְצוֹ בְּסוֹף אֶרֶץ אֱדוֹם, בְּמִזְרָחָהּ שֶׁל אֱדוֹם וּבִדְרוֹמָהּ שֶׁל אֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל:


**To the king of Moav as well.** Whose country bordered the extremity of the land of Edom, east of Edom, and south of Eretz Yisroel.


###### Rashi on Judges 11:17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:17:3)


**וְלֹא אָבָה.** מֹשֶׁה רְמָזָהּ בַּתּוֹרָה (דברים ב:כט): כַּאֲשֶׁר עָשׂוּ לִי בְּנֵי עֵשָׂו הַיּוֹשְׁבִים בְּשֵׂעִיר וְהַמּוֹאָבִים הַיּוֹשְׁבִים בְּעָר, כְּשֵׁם שֶׁאֱדוֹם לֹא נְתָנוּם לַעֲבֹר, כָּךְ מוֹאָב לֹא נְתָנוּם לַעֲבֹר:


**And he refused.** Moshe alludes to this in the Torah, "As perpetrated against me by the Bnei Eisov residing at Sair, and the Moavites residing at Ar."² Just as Edom refused them permission to pass, so did Moav refuse them permission to pass.

footnotes:
² Devarim, 2:29.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on Judges 11:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:18:1)


**וַיֵּלֶךְ בַּמִּדְבָּר.** מִן הַמַּעֲרָב לַמִּזְרָח, בְּמֵיצַר דְּרוֹמִי שֶׁל אֱדוֹם וּמוֹאָב:


**They then continued through the wilderness** from west to east, along the southern borders of Edom and Moav.


###### Rashi on Judges 11:18:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:18:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:18:2)


**וַיָּסָב אֶת אֶרֶץ אֱדוֹם.** כָּל דְּרוֹמָהּ:


**Circumvented the land of Edom.** All of its southern border.


###### Rashi on Judges 11:18:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:18:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:18:3)


**וְאֶת אֶרֶץ מוֹאָב.** כָּל דְּרוֹמָהּ, וּכְשֶׁהִגִּיעַ לְמִקְצוֹעַ דְּרוֹמִית מִזְרָחִית, פָּנָה אֶל הַצָּפוֹן לִסְבּוֹב אֶת מֵיצַר הַמִּזְרָחִי:


**And the land of Moav.** All of its southern border. When they reached the southeastern corner, they angled northward along the periphery of the eastern border.


###### Rashi on Judges 11:18:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:18:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:18:4)


**וַיָּבֹא מִמִּזְרַח שֶׁמֶשׁ לְאֶרֶץ מוֹאָב וַיַּחֲנוּן בְּעֵבֶר אַרְנוֹן.** לְסוֹף מִזְרָחָהּ שֶׁל אֶרֶץ מוֹאָב, וְשָׁם הִתְחִילָה אֶרֶץ סִיחוֹן וְעוֹג, וְכָבְשׁוּ עֵבֶר הַיַּרְדֵּן בְּמִזְרַח אֶרֶץ כְּנַעַן וְעָבְרוּ הַיַּרְדֵּן, וְנִכְנְסוּ לָהּ לְאֶרֶץ כְּנַעַן דֶּרֶךְ הַמִּזְרָח:


**Arrived at the east of the land of Moav, and camped opposite Arnon.** At the end of the eastern border of the land of Moav. This was the starting point of the lands of Sichon and Og. They conquered the trans-Yardeinian territory east of the land of Canaan, crossed the Yardein, and invaded the land of Canaan by the eastern route.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on Judges 11:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:22:1)


**וַיִּירְשׁוּ וְגוֹ׳ מֵאַרְנוֹן וְעַד הַיַּבֹּק.** שֶׁאַתָּה אוֹמֵר שֶׁהוּא שֶׁלְּךָ (פסוק יג), הֵם מִסִּיחוֹן לָקְחוּ:


**They took possession, etc., from Arnon to the Yabok.** Which you claim as yours, they annexed from Sichon.


###### Rashi on Judges 11:22:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:22:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:22:2)


**וּמִן הַמִּדְבָּר וְעַד הַיַּרְדֵּן.** מִדְבָּרוֹת הָיוּ שָׁם לִפְנֵי הַיַּרְדֵּן:


**From the wilderness to the Yardein.** There was a wilderness area adjoining the Yardein.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on Judges 11:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:26:1)


**עַל יְדֵי אַרְנוֹן.** אֵצֶל אַרְנוֹן, שֶׁהָיוּ מִמּוֹאָב, וְיָדְעוּ שֶׁלֹּא הָיָה לָהֶם לַעֲבֹר עֲלֵיהֶם, שֶׁהֲרֵי מִסִּיחוֹן כְּבָשׁוּם (במדבר כא:כד), שֶׁלְּקָחָם מִמֶּלֶךְ מוֹאָב (שם שם כו):


**Adjoining [lit. by the hands of] Arnon.** Near Arnon. These cities had belonged to Moav, but they recognized that they could not lay claim to them, since they were confiscated from Sichon, who had annexed them from the king of Moav.


###### Rashi on Judges 11:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:26:2)


**שְׁלֹשׁ מֵאוֹת שָׁנָה.** מִשֶּׁכָּבְשׁוּ אֶת הָאָרֶץ בִּימֵי יְהוֹשֻׁעַ עַד יִפְתָּח מִכָּאן יֵשׁ לִלְמוֹד שְׁנוֹת הַשּׁוֹפְטִים הַנִּזְכָּרִים עַד הֵנָּה, אִם שְׁנֵי הַשִּׁעְבּוּד שֶׁל כָּל אוּמָּה נִמְנִים בְּתוֹךְ יְמֵי הַשּׁוֹפֵט אִם לָאו. שָׁנִינוּ בְּסֵדֶר עוֹלָם (פרק יב): יְהוֹשֻׁעַ פִּרְנֵס אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל עֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁמוֹנֶה שָׁנָה, וְאֵין לוֹ מִקְרָא מִמִּי לִלְמוֹד. עָתְנִיאֵל (לעיל ג:יא), אַרְבָּעִים שָׁנָה, וּשְׁנֵי שִׁעְבּוּד שֶׁל כּוּשַׁן רִשְׁעָתַיִם (שם שם ח) בִּכְלָלָם. אַחֲרָיו אֵהוּד (שם שם ל), שְׁמוֹנִים שָׁנָה, שְׁמוֹנֶה עֶשְׂרֵה שָׁנִים שִׁעְבּוּד שֶׁל עֶגְלוֹן (שם שם יד) בְּתוֹכָם, הֲרֵי מֵאָה אַרְבָּעִים וּשְׁמוֹנֶה שָׁנָה. דְּבוֹרָה (שם ה:לא) אַרְבָּעִים שָׁנָה, וּשְׁנֵי שִׁעְבּוּד יָבִין (שם ד:ג) בְּתוֹכָם, הֲרֵי מֵאָה שְׁמוֹנִים וּשְׁמוֹנֶה. אַחֲרָיו, שֶׁבַע שָׁנִים שֶׁל שִׁעְבּוּד מִדְיָן (שם ו:א), וְאַרְבָּעִים שֶׁל גִּדְעוֹן (שם ח:כח), וְשָׁלֹשׁ שֶׁל אֲבִימֶלֶךְ (שם ט:כב), הֲרֵי מָאתַיִם שְׁלֹשִׁים וּשְׁמוֹנֶה שָׁנִים. אַחֲרָיו, עֶשְׂרִים וְשָׁלֹשׁ שֶׁל תּוֹלָע (שם י:ב), וְעֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁתַּיִם שֶׁל יָאִיר (שם י:ג), אֶלָּא שֶׁעָלְתָה שָׁנָה אַחַת לִשְׁנֵיהֶם, וּשְׁמוֹנֶה עֶשְׂרֵה שֶׁל בְּנֵי עַמּוֹן (שם שם ח) עַד שֶׁלֹּא בָּא יִפְתָּח, הֲרֵי שְׁלֹשׁ מֵאוֹת:


**Three centuries** from the conquest of the land in the days of Yehoshua, until Yiftoch. This teaches us concerning the years of the Judges mentioned until now—whether the years of subjugation by the nations are included in the reckoning of the days of the Judge, or not.³ We learn in Seder Olam:⁴ Yehoshua directed Yisroel for twenty eight years—I am not aware of any Scriptural derivation for this. Asniel for forty years,⁵ the years of subjugation by Cushan Rishasaim are included.⁶ Afterward, Ehud for eighty years,⁷ with the eighteen years of subjugation by Eglon⁸ included. This totals one hundred forty eight years. Devorah for forty years,⁹ with the years of subjugation by Yavin¹⁰ included, totaling one hundred eighty eight years. Afterward, seven years of subjugation by Midyon,¹¹ forty years of Gidon,¹² and¹³ three of Avimelech, totaling two hundred thirty eight years. Afterward, twenty three years of Tola¹⁴ and twenty two of Yair,¹⁵ with one year overlapping both, and eighteen years of the Bnei Ammon¹⁶ until Yiftoch's appearance. This totals three hundred.

footnotes:
³ The three hundred year total is only accurate if certain of the years of subjugation are included in the years of the judge, and certain years are not, as Rashi explains below.
⁴ Ch. 12.
⁶ Eight years. (3:8).
¹⁰ Twenty years. (4:3.)



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on Judges 11:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:29:1)


**עָבַר בְּנֵי עַמּוֹן.** אֶל בְּנֵי עַמּוֹן:


**He crossed to the Bnei Ammon.** "To" the Bnei Ammon.



### Verse 33

###### Rashi on Judges 11:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:33:1)


**אָבֵל כְּרָמִים.** (תרגום:) מֵישַׁר כַּרְמַיָּא:


**The Vineyard plains.** The plain of the vineyards.¹⁷

footnotes:
¹⁷ This is Targum Yonasan’s translation.



### Verse 35

###### Rashi on Judges 11:35:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:35:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:35:1)


**הַכְרֵעַ הִכְרַעְתִּנִי.** לְשׁוֹן נְקֵבָה, וְדוֹמֶה לוֹ (שיר השירים ד:ט): לִבַּבְתִּנִי אֲחוֹתִי כַלָּה:


**You are among those who ruined me.** This is the feminine usage.¹⁸” vocalization under the **ת**, is generally translated as referring to the first person neuter, “I brought to the knees.” Here, however, the translation is the feminine “you brought to the knees.” Similarly, "You captured my heart, my sister bride."¹⁹” vocalization under the the **ת**, is translated as the feminine “you captured my heart.”

footnotes:
¹⁸ הִכְרַעְתִּנִי, with the “cherik
¹⁹ Shir Hashirim, 4:9. Here, too, לִבַּבֽתִּנִי, with the “cherik


###### Rashi on Judges 11:35:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:35:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:35:2)


**הָיִית בְּעוֹכְרִי.** כָּל דָּמִי נֶעְכָּר, קִפַּחְתְּ אֶת רַגְלִי:


**You have surely brought me to my knees** All my blood is in turmoil, You have taken away my feet.



### Verse 37

###### Rashi on Judges 11:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:37:1)


**וְיָרַדְתִּי עַל הֶהָרִים.** לְשׁוֹן יְלָלָה הוּא, וְדוּגְמָתוֹ (ישעיהו טו:ג): עַל גַּגּוֹתֶיהָ וּבִרְחוֹבוֹתֶיהָ כֻּלֹּה יְיֵלִיל יָרַד בִּבְכִי, הַמִּתְעַצֵּב בִּבְכִי עַד שֶׁגּוּפוֹ מִשְׁתַּבֵּר. וּבְמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה דּוֹרֵשׁ רַבִּי תַּנְחוּמָא (בחוקותי ה): **עַל הֶהָרִים.** לִפְנֵי סַנְהֶדְרִין, שֶׁמָּא יִמְצְאוּ פֶּתַח לְנִדְרְךָ:


**To grieve on the hills.** This word refers to bewailing. ²⁰ Similarly, "On its roofs and thoroughfares, total wailing, weeping grief."²¹ Weeping so intense that it causes a physical breakdown.²² Midrash Agada: R' Tanchuma expounded, On the hills; before the Sanhedrin—perhaps they may find a loophole in your vow.²³

footnotes:
²⁰ Not, as translated elsewhere, “To descend,” but “To grieve.”
²¹ Yeshaya, 15:3.
²² רד may also be translated as “crushing.” See Yeshaya, 45:1.
²³ Yalkut, 67.



### Verse 38

###### Rashi on Judges 11:38:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:38:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:38:1)


**וַתֵּבְךְּ עַל בְּתוּלֶיהָ.** מִדְּלָא כְתִיב עַל בְּתוּלוֹתֶיהָ, מַשְׁמַע בְּתוּלִים מַמָּשׁ:


**Wept over her maidenhood.** Since it is not written "over her maidens," the inference is, over her actual maidenhood.



### Verse 39

###### Rashi on Judges 11:39:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:39:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:39:1)


**וַתְּהִי חֹק.** גָּזְרוּ שֶׁלֹּא יַעֲשֶׂה אָדָם עוֹד כֵּן, שֶׁאִלּוּ הָלַךְ אֵצֶל פִּנְחָס, אוֹ הוּא בָּא אֶצְלוֹ, הָיָה מַתִּיר לוֹ נִדְרוֹ, עָמְדוּ בִּגְדֻלָּתָן, וּמִבֵּין שְׁנֵיהֶם אָבְדָה, וְנִפְרַע מֵהֶם, פִּנְחָס נִסְתַּלְּקָה שְׁכִינָה הֵימֶנּוּ, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים (א ט:כ): לְפָנִים ה׳ עִמּוֹ, לָמַדְנוּ שֶׁבְּסוֹפוֹ אֵינוֹ עִמּוֹ, וְיִפְתָּח הוּכָּה בִּשְׁחִין וְנִפּוּל אֵבָרִים, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (לקמן יב:ז): וַיִּקָּבֵר בְּעָרֵי גִלְעָד. וְיֵשׁ עוֹד לִפְתֹּר: **וַתְּהִי חֹק בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל.** מְחֻבָּר לְמִקְרָא שֶׁלְּאַחֲרָיו:


**She became the subject of a law.** Legislation was enacted prohibiting any repetition of this, for, had he approached Pinchos, or had Pinchos approached him, he would have released him from his vow.²⁴ But they refused to humble themselves,²⁵ and were, thus, both responsible for her ruin. They were punished. The Divine Presence departed from Pinchos, as it is said in Divrei Hayamim,²⁶ "Adonoy was with him previously." We learn that he was not with him later. And Yiftoch was afflicted with ulcerations and the loss of his limbs, as it is said,²⁷ "He was buried in the cities of Gilod."²⁸ Another interpretation: "She became the subject of a law in Yisroel" relates to the following passage.²⁹

footnotes:
²⁴ By informing him that a human being cannot be designated as an offering.
²⁵ Pinchas, because it was Yiftoch who required his services; Yiftoch, because he was the supreme commander. (Yalkut, 68)
²⁶ Divrei Hayamim 1, 9:20.
²⁷ Below, 12:7.
²⁸ Individual limbs, like the entire body, require burial. The plural usage, “cities”, teaches that his burial took place in a number of cities—wherever his limbs fell. (Yalkut, ibid.)
²⁹ She was the subject of a law requiring the daughters of Yisroel to visit her yearly.

**מִיָּמִים יָמִימָה תֵּלַכְנָה וְגוֹ׳.** הַדָּבָר הַזֶּה קָבְעוּ עֲלֵיהֶם לְחֹק:


**Journeyed annually.** This was established as a law.³⁰

³⁰ This follows Rashi’s second interpretation of v. 39. See fn.29.


###### Rashi on Judges 11:40:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:40:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/11:40:2)


**לְתַנּוֹת.** לְקוֹנֵן:


**To grieve.** To weep.



## Chapter 12


### Verse 1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/12:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/12:1:1)


**וַיַּעֲבֹר צָפוֹנָה.** וַיַּעֲבֹר אֶת הַיַּרְדֵּן, וְהָלְכוּ לִצְפוֹן עֵבֶר הַיַּרְדֵּן, אֶל הַגִּלְעָד:


**And crossed northward.** They crossed the Yardein and travelled north in trans-Yardein to Gilod.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Judges 12:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/12:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/12:4:1)


**כִּי אָמְרוּ פְּלִיטֵי אֶפְרַיִם אַתֶּם גִּלְעָד.** פְּחוּתִים שֶׁבְּאֶפְרַיִם הָיוּ מְבַזִּים אֶת הַגִּלְעָד, וְאוֹמְרִים לָהֶם: מָה אַתֶּם גִּלְעָד חֲשׁוּבִים בְּתוֹךְ אֶפְרַיִם וּבְתוֹךְ מְנַשֶּׁה, כֵּן תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן:


**Since the contemptibles of Ephraim had said,** "What are you ,Gilod?" The lowliest in Ephraim would mock Gilod, saying, "Of what significance are you, Gilod, among Ephraim and among Menasheh?" This is Targum Yonasan's interpretation.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on Judges 12:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/12:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/12:5:1)


**וַיִּלְכֹּד גִּלְעָד אֶת מַעְבְּרוֹת וְגוֹ׳.** הָיוּ שׁוֹמְרִים בִּמְקוֹם הַמַּעְבָּרוֹת:


**The Gilodites captured the Yardein crossings.** They posted sentries at the crossing areas.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on Judges 12:6:1

**Say Shiboles please.** May I cross this river flow?¹

###### Rashi on Judges 12:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/12:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/12:6:2)

**They were not proficient in proper pronunciation.** They were speech deficient.

###### Rashi on Judges 12:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/12:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Judges/r/12:8:1)

###### Rashi on Judges 13:7:1

### Verse 8

###### Rashi on Judges 13:8:1
